Owner changes for related work: `parent-atlas-gpu-mini-fabric-01`, `parent-atlas-ace-radix-residency`, `parent-atlas-cuda134-tensorrt-rtx-lane` — extend those, do not fork a peer owner.

## 3. Standing rules (kept in CLAUDE.md as a short section)

Env isolation; absolute-path Python for WSL envs; no second RAPIDS env / no 26.06→26.08 upgrade without proven unusability; Ampere 8 GiB alignment (no concurrent cuDF/cuGraph/cuVS + cuTile + large decoder; dims divisible by 8; RMM capped pool); LEVEL 1→2→3 ladder; "ACE" naming collision (NVIDIA cuVS ACE ≠ Atlas ACE); radix/CUB is a backend under ACE/BitFrost, never a retrieval vote; no cuTile for graph traversal first; dependency install requires proven capability gap; no stack moves to CUDA 13.4 except TensorRT-RTX challenger; LibTorch target 13.2.2+; `nvidia-smi` for VRAM go/no-go; Tang machinery is the owner, do not build a second.

## 5. OpenSpec compaction + retrieval + tournament pipeline — alignment and gaps (2026-10-03)

Design (operator): classify current/stale/superseded → task cards → report manifests → compaction → chunk+embed → NLP/ontology features → candidate feature matrix → deterministic incumbent + challenger tournament → current work queue → ACE/BitFrost/Valkey hot routing → agent executes a selected task. Storage split: Postgres = canonical task/evidence identity + revisions; repo/.okf = small manifests/receipts; SeaweedFS = large immutable replay artifacts; Qdrant = derived projection; Valkey = disposable hot routing; TurboVec = challenger; GPU KMeans = optional routing feature. `tasks.md` stays authority; every derived record carries `canonicalAuthority:false`; a model or tournament winner never edits task status.

**Measured against `docs/reports/openspec-workboard-v1.json` (generated 2026-09-26, 9,619 tasks) and the `openspec-evidence-*-v1.json` stage reports — read-only:**

| Design stage | Already exists (reuse) | Missing / misaligned | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Classify | `executionState` ∈ DONE 6,369 / ACTIONABLE 2,431 / WAITING_ON_DEPENDENCY 764 / SUPERSEDED_OR_HISTORICAL 53 / INVARIANT 2; `completionTracking` | No STALE / ORPHAN / REVIEW_REQUIRED / CLAIMED_COMPLETE; all 6,369 DONE are checkbox claims (`baselineCompletedNotIndividuallyObserved:6369`). **`timestampMethod` is `FILESYSTEM_MTIME` for all 9,619 tasks** — fine as a display hint, must not drive staleness | **WB-STATE-01** evidence-based states from stableKey + blockHash + source hash + receipt revision + `superseded_by`; mtime never decides; DONE without independent proof → CLAIMED_COMPLETE |
| 2 Task identity | **WB-KEY-01 RESULT (read-only, 2026-10-03): identity is already sound.** `taskKey` = `change:<line>` is a display coordinate only. Real identity = `taskIdentity` (`scripts/atlas/lib/wfu-metadata.mjs:119-125`): `logicalTaskKey` = declared ID (`change:ID`) when unique; else `migrationKey` = `stableKey` = `change#<16-hex title hash>` (not line-based, 9,619 distinct); `taskRevision` = `blockHash`. Basis counts: DECLARED_ID 2,785; DECLARED_ID_SECTION_QUALIFIED 99; AMBIGUOUS_DECLARED_ID 155; MIGRATION_TITLE_HASH 6,580. **Fresh dry-run (2026-10-03):** 2,057 OPEN title-hash tasks; 2,057 suggestions; zero source drift/missing files; 48 files; 519,404-byte patch generated under `%TEMP%`; `tasksFilesEdited=0`, `writesPerformed=false`. Stale persisted Workboard dry-run was rejected (1,008 drift skips); only the fresh in-memory snapshot result is current. | Remaining: (a) 155 ambiguous declared IDs; (b) 16 duplicate-`blockHash` groups: 14 single-change boilerplate duplicates + one 13-change group of the "CANONICAL-IDENTITY-V1 POINTER (2026-09-21)" line + one 2-change group — `stableKey` stays distinct so no identity collision, but `blockHash` must not be used alone as a unique key; (c) `wfu-v1` declarations (`depends/est/reads/writes`) exist on only 4 tasks, so `readSet`/`writeSet` are effectively absent; (d) proposals require operator review before any `tasks.md` edit. | **WB-KEY-01:** dry-run `DRY_RUN_PROVEN`; apply `NOT_AUTHORIZED / NOT_DONE`. Resolve ambiguous IDs; key any `blockHash` join by (`change`, `blockHash`, occurrence); do NOT build a second identity scheme. |
| 2 Task card | `dependsOn`, `unlocks`, `declaredDependencies`, `effectiveDependencies`, `proofReceipt`, `proofLevel`, `writeRisk`, `mutationClass`, `gateState`, `fanoutCount`, `criticalPathRank`, `artifactOutputs`, `owner`, `canonicalOwner` | No compact `OpenSpecTaskCardV1` projection; no `readSet`/`writeSet`, `domainHints`/`conceptHints`, `summary`, `evidenceRefs` | **WB-CARD-01** derive the card as a projection of `taskInventory` (no second parser); `canonicalAuthority:false` |
| 3 Report manifests | `sourceFileHashes` for tasks.md sources only | No `ReportArtifactManifestV1` (sha256, producer/revision, storageClass HOT_LOCAL/WARM_LOCAL/COLD_SEAWEEDFS, replayRequired, sensitivityClass, seaweedRef); report corpus size per operator audit (1,255 reports, 14.34 GB, 325 > 10 MB) is unverified here (`du`/`find` over `docs/reports` exceeded 120 s) | **WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01** manifest first; **WB-SEAWEED-01** copy → checksum readback → manifest → replay verify → only then optional local removal; one-file canary to a separate `atlas-archive` bucket (not `legal-evidence`); never bulk delete/move the 325 large files |
| 4 Summaries | Chunk-summary admission contracts (`chunk-summary-admission-writer-v1`, `summary-quality-v1`, Ornith adapter) | No task/report `TaskSummaryV1` (model + prompt revision, unresolvedClaims, blockers, proposedNextGate, evidenceRefs); no deterministic-first extractor/threshold gate | **WB-SUMMARY-01** deterministic extraction first (title, checkbox, IDs, deps, paths, receipts, status words, gates, error classes); call Ornith only for over-threshold/ambiguous/multi-update/unclear-supersession blocks; reuse the existing admission contract; model never changes status |
| 5 Chunk + embed | `openspec-evidence-embedding-plan-v1` = `MODEL_ARTIFACT_UNPROVEN` | Hierarchy L0 card / L1 block / L2 receipt synopsis / L3 report section / L4 raw pointer not defined; embed compact records only, never 10–100 MB reports; also blocked by the unprefixed raw embedding callers (§4) | **WB-EMBED-01** gated on the embedding-artifact gate; expand L0→L4 only on demand |
| 6 NLP / ontology | `propose-openspec-langextract-candidates-v1.mjs`, `openspec-langextract-proposals-v1.json`; `mutationClass`, `writeRisk`, `gateState` already present | Task-level feature set (domain, component, language, schema risk, GPU need, human-approval need, production-write risk, external dependency, test need, identity/lineage/retrieval/cache dependency) not produced; ontology resolution stays separate from extraction | **WB-FEAT-01** grounded tuples (span-backed) → canonical domain resolution via existing `atlas_domain_ontology`/OKF; no new tuple table |
| 7 Task graph | `dependsOn`/`unlocks`/`laneDependencies`/work packages; NetworkX↔cuGraph parity oracle | Edge kinds TOUCHES/REQUIRES/VALIDATED_BY/EVIDENCED_BY/BELONGS_TO/BLOCKED_BY/SUPERSEDED_BY not materialised; no dependency depth, downstream-blocked count, betweenness, PPR, community features | **WB-GRAPH-01** compute features with the existing NetworkX oracle pattern; features only — PageRank never decides task truth |
| 8 Task feature matrix | `criticalPathRank`, `fanoutCount`, `proofLevel`, `writeRisk` | No `CandidateFeatureMatrixV1` for tasks (rows = current open tasks; goalRank, selectionEligible, remainingRequiredGates, unblocksGateCount, proofCoverage, stalenessAge, affectedFileCount, schema/production/human-approval risk, domainAffinity, semanticQueryScore, graphPpr, historicalSuccessRate, estimatedCost, resourcePressure) | **WB-MATRIX-01** one `CandidateOrdinalMap` checksum shared by all columns; Arrow/mmap artifact + JSON receipt, not a table |
| 9 Tournament | Incumbent = existing workboard `ordering`; `scripts/atlas/adapt-openspec-controller-to-ranker-v3.mjs` (+ `actionable-workboard-v3.json`); Tang low-rank challenger exists (`low_rank.py`) but its plan is `BLOCKED_EMBEDDING_ARTIFACT_GATE` | No `TaskRecommendationV1` (taskKey, recommenderId/revision, score, featureRefs, explanationRefs, `canonicalAuthority:false`); no shadow-winner comparison harness. Participants: A deterministic order, B low-rank, C semantic/query affinity, D graph/dependency, E GEPA/agent policy (later), F XGBoost (later) | **WB-TOURNAMENT-01** compare in shadow; winner → validation → *suggestion* only; never auto-edits `tasks.md`. Extend ranker-v3, do not add a peer ranker |
| 10 Hot routing | ACE/BitFrost/Valkey contracts exist; `context-manifest` = `BLOCKED_RETRIEVAL_GATE`; admission verdict `NOT_SAFE_TO_PROJECT` | Nothing to warm until identity/admission passes; TurboVec plan `BLOCKED_FROZEN_TRUTH_SET_GATE`; GPU plan `BLOCKED_PROJECTION_PARITY_GATE` | Keep gated; no Valkey task-routing writes until the BitFrost canary tasks (other change) pass |
| 11 Postgres identity | `ledger-import-plan` = `BLOCKED_IMPORT_PLAN`; `ledger-readback` = `UNPROVEN_NO_LIVE_DATABASE` | Canonical task/evidence identity in Postgres not imported or read back | **WB-LEDGER-01** after WB-KEY-01; disposable-Postgres rehearsal first |

**Alignment verdict.** Aligned with existing governance: `tasks.md` authority, `canonicalAuthority:false` on derived records, model-never-edits-status, storage split, BitFrost/Valkey disposable. Misaligned today: (a) staleness/ordering metadata is mtime-only (`timestampMethod` = `FILESYSTEM_MTIME` on all 9,619), (b) 6,580 tasks have only a title-hash identity and 155 have ambiguous declared IDs (identity design itself is sound — see WB-KEY-01 result; the earlier "`taskKey` line-based / no declared IDs" claim was overstated), (c) `readSet`/`writeSet` declarations exist on only 4 tasks, (d) DONE conflates "checked" with "proven". Every downstream stage (embedding, low-rank, TurboVec, GPU, synthesis, ledger) is blocked at an upstream gate, so the buildable-now order is CPU/deterministic only: **WB-KEY-01 → WB-STATE-01 → WB-CARD-01 → WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 → (SeaweedFS one-file canary) → WB-GRAPH-01 → WB-MATRIX-01 → WB-TOURNAMENT-01 in shadow**; embedding/summary/ledger/hot-routing wait on their gates. Regenerate the workboard (`npm run atlas:docs:workboard`) before acting — the snapshot is a week old.

- [ ] **VITERBI-EVAL-01** frozen query→tool gold set (the missing `qualityEvaluation` precondition, 9/9) and a shadow tournament: incumbent regex ranker vs semantic selector (`runtime-mcp-tool-selector.mjs`) vs Viterbi bridge; metrics recall@1/3 and wrong-tool rate; reuse the WB-TOURNAMENT-01 harness shape, do not add a peer ranker.

- [ ] **VITERBI-CALLERS-01** caller migration evidence + deterministic tests (7 missing) + identity/revision propagation (6 missing); only then reassess `DEFER-VIBRETI-01`.
- Hard rules: proposal-only, read-only tools only, never executes or edits; register any agent-facing selector in `ACPToolRegistry.ts`; a Viterbi winner never edits `tasks.md` or tool permissions.

**Label every hit** in the census with one of `CANONICAL_OWNER | LIVE_CONSUMER | CHALLENGER | FIXTURE_ONLY | LEGACY_COMPAT | DORMANT | DEAD`; add no new implementation until each capability has one owner entry.

**Adopted completion order** (reconciles the review's 10 steps with §4–§9; I had proposed regenerating the workboard and a SeaweedFS canary earlier — both now explicitly later, steps 4 and 7):
1. **EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01** — all query/document embeddings pass one prefix-aware canonical adapter (not merely "768-dim"). First bounded slices route TRACE MCP and `build-mcp-tool-manifest-packets.mjs` through `/api/embed`, and six SvelteKit query routes through `executeEmbeddingInputV1`, explicitly preserving `unprompted_legacy`. The tool-manifest builder also carries the API recipe receipt into Qdrant payloads. The active `retrieval/embedding-service.ts` `dense_768` compatibility path now also uses `executeEmbeddingInputV1` in `unprompted_legacy` mode; its focused regression preserves the raw request and exact-768 failure behavior (15 adapter/retrieval tests pass). This does not qualify mixed corpus recipes or close the gate: classify/migrate remaining direct callers and prove executor parity before persistence/promotion; then add EMB-CALLERS-GUARD-01.
2. **DB-READY-TRISTATE-01** — `STARTING | READY | UNAVAILABLE`; SQLSTATE `57P03`/`pg_isready` exit 1 → STARTING; STARTING never creates an agentic-repair task; Playwright global-setup waits on `pg_isready → SELECT 1 → SHOW server_version`.
3. **MULTIHOP-FILL-01** — prove neighbor population lineage and bounded k-hop rerank; no new orchestrator.
4. **WB-SUPERSESSION-01** — harden `build-openspec-workboard-v1.mjs` classification with `supersededBy`/replacement-receipt/`blockHash`+revision evidence; reviewed supersession edges (WB-COMPACT-02 TaskCard corpus); mtime never decides. Regenerate the workboard only after this is designed.

Until that receipt contract is wired and independently validated, candidates remain `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, `canonicalAuthority:false`, and `retrievalSuppressed:false`.

**ACE catalog registry (Pokédex-style) field split** — `canonicalId = packetKey` (actual identity); `titleId` = catalog coordinate; display name; domains/types/capabilities/tools; `dependsOn` (move prerequisites); feature vector/stats; `supersededBy` (evolution lineage); centroid/community routing; tier HOT/WARM/COLD (party/box/archive). Catalog fields are routing/display, never identity.

**EMB-RECIPE-01 — first result (2026-10-03, `audit-embedding-recipe-census-v1.mjs --per-stratum=10`, read-only; receipt `docs/reports/embedding-recipe-census-v1.json`; sample is tiny, threshold cos ≥ 0.995, UNKNOWN preserved, not guessed):**

| Stratum (population) | `content_embedding` (halfvec 768) | `content_embedding_768` (vector 768) |
|---|---|---|
| both columns populated (739) | `title: {relative_path} \| text:` 4, UNKNOWN 4, raw 2 | UNKNOWN 10 |
| `content_embedding` only (54,430) | `title_relative_path` 6, UNKNOWN 3, raw 1 | — |
| `content_embedding_768` only (219,259) | — | **raw 10/10** |
| tagged `embedding_model = embeddinggemma:latest:eg-task-prefix-v1` (52,364) | `title_relative_path` 7, UNKNOWN 3 | — |

Findings: (1) the corpus is **not one recipe** — `content_embedding` is mostly `title: {relative_path} | text:` with raw and unidentified (UNKNOWN) rows mixed in; `content_embedding_768` is raw. (2) Even the rows **tagged `eg-task-prefix-v1` match the `title: {relative_path}` recipe, not `title: none | text:`** which is the document prefix `embedding-contract-768.ts` documents — a document formatter built from that contract would not reproduce the stored vectors. (3) The two 768 columns hold different recipes, so a query embedded with any single recipe is only consistent with part of the corpus. **Consequence: do not pick or enforce a prefix recipe from the contract file alone.** Next: **EMB-RECIPE-02** rerun with a larger `--per-stratum` (≥100) and classify the UNKNOWN rows (different text normalization / truncation / model revision?) before choosing the canonical recipe; the adapter (EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01) must record the recipe id in every receipt.

**EMB-RECIPE-02 — n=100/stratum result (supersedes the n=10 table above; n=10 receipt kept as `docs/reports/embedding-recipe-census-v1.n10.json`):**

| Stratum (population) | `content_embedding` | `content_embedding_768` |
|---|---|---|
| both columns (739) | `title_relative_path` 55, UNKNOWN 24, raw 21 | UNKNOWN 100/100 |
| `content_embedding` only (54,430) | `title_relative_path` 58, UNKNOWN 36, raw 6 | — |
| `content_embedding_768` only (219,259) | — | raw 100/100 |
| tagged `eg-task-prefix-v1` (52,364) | `title_relative_path` 63, UNKNOWN 37 | UNKNOWN 2 (where populated) |

Confirms the n=10 direction at 10× the sample: `content_embedding` ≈ 55–63% `title: {relative_path} | text:`, **24–37% UNKNOWN** (a large share, not noise), raw is a minority (6–21%); `content_embedding_768` is raw where it is the only column but **UNKNOWN for every one of the 100 both-column rows** (a different text or recipe than raw). The tagged-prefix rows still do not match `title: none`. **EMB-RECIPE-03** (next): classify the UNKNOWN rows (text normalization/truncation/model revision) before choosing a canonical recipe; until then no prefix may be enforced from the contract file.

## 11. Gemma4 → Ornith naming: policy, census, migration lanes (2026-10-03, `git grep` over tracked files)

**Answer: not a blanket rename — partly legacy, partly still-real Gemma4, partly functional.** "Gemma4" currently names four different things; only one of them should change.

| Role | What it is | Census | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Chat/synthesis/tool runtime** (model id strings sent to llama-server `:8090`) | `gemma4-rotorquant:latest`, `gemma4-legal-iq4xs-direct.gguf`, `'hforf'` hard-coded as the `model` | **70 non-spec files** in `src/lib/server`, `src/routes`, `src/mcp` (top: `mcp/server.ts` 5, `features/cases/{web-research-crawler,hypergraph-4d}.ts` 5 each, `inference/inference-router.ts` 4, `ai/tiered-llm-cache.ts` 3, `atlas/phase101-parent-atlas-packetizer.mjs` 3) | **MIGRATE to the resolver, not to another literal.** `ai/model-ids.ts` already has `SERVER_CHAT_MODEL`/`TURBOQUANT_MODEL = 'ornith-1.5-9b'` and `llama-server-model-resolver.ts` resolves the served model; hard-coding `ornith-1.5-9b` everywhere just moves the staleness. |
| **B. Real Gemma4 artifacts (keep the name)** | VLM lane `:8085` (`SERVER_VLM_MODEL = 'gemma4-rotorquant:latest'`, `gemma4:e4b`, 8 files); client ONNX `static/gemma4_e2b_onnx` + `triton-trt-llm/models/gemma4_e2b_onnx` (18 files); `AtlasGemmaRank*` (Gemma4-assistant derived); `scripts/unsloth-training/*gemma4*` (9, trains Gemma4); `gemma4-offload` MCP name (5; documented compatibility alias) | — | **KEEP.** Renaming these would misdescribe the artifact. EmbeddingGemma is a separate Gemma-family embedding model, unrelated to this rename. |
| **C. Historical records** | `docs/reports/*`, `docs/reports/sessions/*` (10), `sveltekit-frontend/docs/obsidian-vault/Files/*` (8), receipts, archive notes | 214 tracked file names contain `gemma4` (largest dirs: `scripts/atlas` 20, `docs/reports` 13) | **NEVER rename** (archive-not-delete; receipts must stay byte-stable). |
| **D. Env var names** | `GEMMA4_URL` (73 refs), `GEMMA4_MODEL` (49), `GEMMA4_VLM_MAX_EDGE` (16), `GEMMA4_TIMEOUT_MS` (11), `GEMMA4_ENDPOINT` (6), `GEMMA4_BASE_URL` (4), `GEMMA4_LEGAL_MODEL` (5), … | 147 files | **Alias, don't break:** read a neutral name first and fall back to the old (`LLM_SYNTH_URL ?? GEMMA4_URL`); the VLM-specific `GEMMA4_VLM_*` stay. Pick the neutral names once, in one helper. |
| **E. Identifier/file names** | `gemma4-tool-controller.ts`, `gemma4-agent.ts`, `gemma4-nlp-reranker.ts`, … (~31 files use `runGemma4`/`Gemma4Tool`/`callGemma4`/`gemma4Chat`) | — | **One file at a time, with a re-export shim**, only after a caller audit. Done: tool controller (`runOrnithToolLoop`, alias kept). Prefer **role names** (`synthesis`, `tool-controller`) over `ornith-*` where the code is model-agnostic — Ornith may be swapped again; name it Ornith only where it is Ornith-specific. |

## 13. Valkey centroid index for hashes + pgvector/EmbeddingGemma ranking + SearXNG searches — lane design (2026-10-03; design/tasks only, nothing written)

**Goal (operator request):** quick retrieval from daily Graphify passes — Valkey holds *centroid routing* for content/task/report hashes; pgvector + EmbeddingGemma do the ranking; SearXNG supplies external search results that are admitted into the corpus. Postgres stays truth; Valkey is disposable.

**Query path (one logical `semantic_768` lane; executors are not separate RRF votes):**
`query → (recipe-stamped) EmbeddingGemma 768 → nearest centroid(s) [≤400×768 or 64×768, brute force or cuVS] → bucket of hashes → pgvector HNSW search restricted to the bucket (exact oracle: whole-corpus HNSW / exact scan) → lexical/trigram RRF + optional rerank → ACE`. Centroid routing is a **prefilter that must prove recall against the exact path before it is allowed to skip anything**; failures fall back to the full pgvector search (cache miss ≠ failure).

| Piece | Existing owner (reuse; no new schema/keys) | Status / gate |
|---|---|---|
| Hash → centroid locator in Valkey | key/identity via `AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1` (`CENTROID` kind, `atlas/cache/ace-bitfrost-cache-identity-v1.ts`); artifact `CentroidArtifactV1` + checksum tests; warmers `scripts/atlas/{warm-centroid-cache,project-valkey-centroid-prefill}.mjs` (two — pick one); value = small descriptor `{centroidId, representationRevision, artifactChecksum}`, **never vectors** (wire-format rule) | Valkey is cold (`noeviction`, 1 `bitfrost:*` key); admission `NOT_SAFE_TO_PROJECT`; write needs the BCI canary + operator OK |
| What a "hash" is | task `blockHash`/`taskRevision` (workboard), report `sha256` (ReportArtifactManifestV1), packet digest, SearXNG result content `sha256` of normalized text — each namespaced; **never interchangeable** (CLAUDE.md revision-namespace rule); the hash is a *lookup key*, `packet_key`/`stableKey` stay identity | namespaces defined in WB-KEY/CARD tasks (§5) |
| Centroids | `gpu_cluster_centroids` is stale (64×768, `kmeans_js`, 2026-07-14) with 4 conflicting writers; rebuild with cuVS KMeans (`atlas_compute/cluster_softmax.py::run_cuvs_soft_kmeans`, WSL `atlas-rapids-cu13`) from the **chosen embedding recipe**, revision-qualified; SOM cells are NOT usable (no derivable `somRevision`) | blocked on EMB-RECIPE-03 + CLUSTER-CENTROID-OWNER-01 |
| pgvector ranking | HNSW `codebase_chunk_index_content_hnsw` (halfvec 768, recall@10 .997 vs exact, ~5–30 ms); canonical vector column `content_embedding`; second column `content_embedding_768` holds a different recipe (§10) | one canonical recipe first; filtered/iterative-scan recall unproven |
| EmbeddingGemma ranking | `canonical-embed.ts` / `embedding-provider-v1.ts` / `embedding-contract-768.ts` (+ Go `:8097`); recipe census receipt | EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 (single prefix-aware adapter) |
| SearXNG | container `legal-ai-searxng` (healthy); `mcp/research_tools.ts`, `kag.web_search`, `ldr_research`, `kb.search_external_research` | external acquisition only: `search → normalize → sha256 → dedupe vs corpus → validate → admit → embed (same recipe) → index`; never an internal retrieval lane; keep provider URL/title/snippet/fetch time; MCP-WEB-01 still unverified |

**Tasks**
- [x] **HASHCENT-01** (CREATED 2026-10-03, tests pass; see below) define the descriptor + key through the existing `AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1` (add a `hashNamespace` field only if the schema lacks one); fixture tests for collisions across namespaces and revisions; no Valkey write.
- [ ] **HASHCENT-02** (contract part CREATED 2026-10-03; cuVS build still open, see below) build a revision-qualified `CentroidArtifactV1` from cuVS KMeans over `semantic_768` in the chosen recipe (K by sweep, deterministic seed, receipt with GPU identity); CPU oracle = `atlas_summary_hint_kmeans_v1.py` MiniBatchKMeans.
- [ ] **HASHCENT-03** offline shadow comparison: centroid-routed pgvector search vs whole-corpus HNSW vs exact oracle (`atlas.exact_topk_oracle`) — recall@10/@50, p50/p95 latency, nprobe sweep. Pass bar set before the run; report negative results.
- [ ] **HASHCENT-04** one disposable write → readback → TTL-expiry canary in Valkey (explicit operator authorization; ties to BCI-10); decide `maxmemory-policy`/TTL first.
- [ ] **RANK-EG-01** EmbeddingGemma ranking as one lane: pgvector cosine (executor) + optional rerank executor behind `canonical-rerank-executor.ts`; classify the 13 unclassified reranker files first; no second vote.
- [ ] **WEB-SEARX-01** SearXNG acquisition pipeline over the existing adapter: content-hash dedupe, admission validation, provenance fields, then embed + index; verify `kag.web_search` routing (MCP-WEB-01); results never carry canonical identity.
- **Hard rules:** Postgres first, Valkey after; hashes are lookup keys not identity; descriptors only, vectors never in Valkey JSON/packets; no centroid write while admission is `NOT_SAFE_TO_PROJECT` or before the embedding recipe is fixed; SearXNG output is external evidence that must be admitted before retrieval.

| Area | Existing owner (reuse; do not add a peer) | Finding | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Headroom — LangGraph state budget** | `sveltekit-frontend/src/mcp/langgraph-bridge.ts` (`LangGraphBridge`, `DispatcherStateSchema`, `HeadroomConfigSchema`: `maxStateSize` 32,000 (commented "32KB" but it counts characters), `maxToolResultChars` 12,000, `priorityLanes`, `memoryOverflowBehavior: 'truncate' \| 'summarize' \| 'error'` **default `'truncate'`**) | Contradicts the settled V2 policy in root CLAUDE.md (UTF-8 **byte** budgets, pinned identity/DAG deps/evidence refs/ContextManifest checksums, receipt/artifact refs for large outputs, **fail closed** — no arbitrary JSON truncation or emergency summary); `ensureSchema()` creates a table at runtime (should be migration-owned). The Ornith controller has the same 12,000-char constant and `truncateToolResult` cuts JSON mid-structure | **HEADROOM-V2-01** default → `'error'` (fail closed); byte-based budget; pinned fields never pruned; large results become artifact/receipt refs; move schema to a migration. Keep the bridge a *state admitter* — execution stays in typed TRACE/OaK operators |
| **Headroom — resource (VRAM/RAM)** | `scripts/atlas/assert-resource-headroom-v1.mjs`, `packages/parent-atlas-retrieval/src/bifrost/residency-scheduler.ts`, `parent-atlas/src/core/inference-runtime-selection.ts` | Different meaning of the same word; must size from `nvidia-smi`, not `cudaMemGetInfo` (§ GPU rules) | **HEADROOM-NAME-01** keep two vocabularies: `STATE_BUDGET` (LangGraph) vs `RESOURCE_HEADROOM` (GPU/host); don't merge code |
| **Hashmap cache (process-local)** | `src/lib/server/ace/cache/revision-aware-cache.ts` (`RevisionAwareCache`: `Map<JSON.stringify(AceCacheIdentity), {value, expiresAt}>`, TTL, lazy expiry) and `src/lib/server/cache.ts` (dual memory + Redis) | Revision-qualified keys already — the right L1 for the §13 hash→centroid descriptor. In the lines read there is **no max-entry bound / LRU / hit-miss counters** (verify the rest of the file); a long-lived server could grow it unboundedly | **HASHMAP-01** add size bound + eviction + counters (specs), keep keys = `AceCacheIdentity`; L2 = Valkey via `AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1`; invalidate by revision, never flush a namespace |
| **SearXNG** | container `legal-ai-searxng`; `mcp/research_tools.ts`; `kag.web_search` / `ldr_research` / `kb.search_external_research`; scripts `audit-external-search-result-parity-v1.mjs` (query default `ast-grep tree-sitter`, limit clamped 1–20, writes `docs/reports/external-search-result-parity-v1.json`), `audit-external-doc-observation-parity-v1.mjs`, `fetch-llm-wiki-corpus.mjs`, `ingest-llm-wiki.mjs`, `audit-port-contracts.mjs` | Acquisition + parity auditing exist; no confirmed link from results to an *admission* step before indexing | **SEARX-AUDIT-01** run the parity audit (read-only except its one receipt) and confirm provider URL/title/snippet/fetch-time survive; then WEB-SEARX-01 (§13) |
| **Agentic dense search** | `src/lib/server/retrieval/packet-dense-search.ts` (+`.spec.ts`) = `atlas.packet_dense_search`: (1) `packet-bitmap-prefilter.ts` Postgres bitmap narrowing → (2) `packet-dense-rerank.ts` Qdrant ANN restricted to that set → (3) join-back by `packet_key`/`source_ref` (never `feature_id` alone); reuses `PacketControlWordV1`; bounded in `mcp/read-tool-bounds.ts`; also `retrieval/rrf-integration.ts`, `search/{qdrant-search,turbovec-search}.ts` | It is a **single-shot** pipeline, not agentic: no graph expansion / rerank loop. Its own header says the control-word bit mapping is a best-effort derivation, not an authoritative bit certification | **DENSE-AGENTIC-01** compose, don't build: bounded plan `packet_dense_search → graph.expand_neighborhood (k-hop capped) → search.rerank` using existing MCP tools and the Ornith tool controller's allowlist; depends on MULTIHOP-FILL-01; smoke test with caps (rounds ≤3, k-hop ≤2) |

**Alignment rules:** one hash-keyed L1 (`RevisionAwareCache`) + one Valkey L2 (`AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1`); Headroom never truncates identity or evidence; SearXNG feeds only the admission path; dense search stays one `semantic_768` lane (pgvector exact · Qdrant · cuVS · TurboVec are executors, not extra votes).

### EMB-RECIPE-03 result (2026-10-03) — UNKNOWN rows classified

- **Cause of the 24–37% UNKNOWN in `content_embedding`:** the census compared against untrimmed text; the documented writer `scripts/atlas/reembed-corpus-document-prefix-v1.mjs::formatDocumentPrompt` does `content.trim()` and `title: ${relative_path.trim() || 'none'}`. Census script now adds `raw_trimmed`, `title_none_trimmed`, `title_relative_path_trimmed` and de-duplicates identical texts (rerun n=100/stratum; prior receipt kept as `docs/reports/embedding-recipe-census-v1.pretrim.json`).
- **Rerun histogram:** `content_embedding` — both_columns 76 title-path (24 untrimmed-identical + 52 trimmed), 21 raw, 3 UNKNOWN; ce-only 93 title-path, 6 raw, 1 UNKNOWN; tagged-prefix 99 title-path, 1 UNKNOWN. `content_embedding_768` — ce768-only stratum 100/100 raw; both-column rows 100/100 UNKNOWN (2/2 in tagged stratum).
- **Exploratory probe (17 variants: truncation 500–8000, basename/symbol/path#symbol titles, summary, task prefix) on 60 both-column ce768 vectors:** 60/60 UNKNOWN, best cosine median 0.922. `content_embedding` vs `content_embedding_768` cosine on the same row: 0 of 739 above 0.995 (median 0.90 for rows updated after `embedding_created_at`, 0.96 otherwise), so the 739 both-column ce768 vectors are a third, unidentified recipe (not simple staleness). They are 0.3% of the ce768 population; leave UNKNOWN, do not enforce a recipe on them.
- **Conclusion:** canonical recipes by column are now evidence-backed: `content_embedding` = `title: {relative_path} | text: {content.trim()}`; `content_embedding_768` (219,259 ce768-only rows) = raw. EMB-RECIPE-03 DONE; EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 (single prefix-aware, trim-aware adapter for ~50 callers) is unblocked, and the centroid rebuild is no longer blocked on this item. Retrieval caveat: a query embedded raw vs corpus embedded with `title:` prefix mismatches — the adapter must apply the matching side (document prefix for corpus; query prefix per `embedding-contract-768.ts`) and decide which column serves retrieval.

### EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 — census + step 1 (2026-10-03)

- **Census (src/ only):** ~108 files call an embedding function and ~124 reference ollama `/api/embed(dings)`. At least 9 independent entry points exist: `embedding/embed.ts::embedText/embedTexts` (cache-first facade, L3 Redis + L4 Postgres, breaker+retry; 12 importers), `retrieval/embedding-service.ts::embedQuery` (4 importers, own L1/L2 caches), `batch-embedder.ts` (5 importers), `ai/ollama-client.ts`, `cache/semantic-cache.ts`, `config/ollama.ts`, `evidence/services/embedding.ts`, `grpc/embedding-client.ts::generateEmbedding`, client-side `ai/client-embed.ts`; plus strict lane `embedding/canonical-embed.ts::embedSemantic768Canonical` (llama-server, input-policy-revision stamped). Only 5 files use the contract formatter (`embeddinggemma-task-representation-v1.ts`, `embeddinggemma-task-runtime-v1.ts`, `query-classification-v2.ts`, `semantic-lineage.ts`, the contract itself). Prefix logic already exists in `embedding-contract-768.ts::formatEmbeddingGemmaInput` (trims; `title: none` fallback) — no new formatter needed.
- **Cache safety finding:** the facade caches key on md5 of the exact text, so formatting the prompt BEFORE the facade makes per-role cache keys differ automatically; no cache-key change required for `embed.ts`. `embedding-service.ts` caches (L1/L2) are keyed on the raw query string and would need the same treatment before it gets a role.
- **Step 1 DONE (tests pass 6/6):** `embedTextAs(mode, text, title?)` and `embedTextsAs(mode, texts, titles?)` added to the declared facade `embedding/embed.ts` (mode = `document` | `retrieval_query` | `code_query`); spec `src/lib/server/embedding/embed-roles.spec.ts`. Additive; no existing caller changed; default `embedText` behavior unchanged.
- **Column recipes to mirror:** `content_embedding` corpus = `embedTextAs("document", content, relative_path)`; `content_embedding_768` corpus = plain raw `embedText(content)`.
- **Open (needs operator decision before migrating callers):** which column/collection serves retrieval. Qdrant `codebase_chunks_768` is fed from an unconfirmed column; a raw query against `title:`-prefixed corpus vectors (or the reverse) is a silent mismatch. Next verification (read-only): check which column the Qdrant sync reads and run a recall probe of raw vs `retrieval_query` queries against each column. Then migrate callers by class (queries first), starting with `embed.ts` importers (12), one file at a time, with specs.

### Query-role recall probe (2026-10-03, read-only, n=60 per column, scratchpad `recall-probe*.mjs`, not a committed receipt)

Self-retrieval of the target chunk id (top-10 from the live index; `content_embedding` uses its HNSW index so it is approximate, `content_embedding_768` is an exact scan). Query = chunk summary (ce only; ce768-only rows have no summaries) or the first 200 chars of content (both columns).

| Column (corpus recipe) | Query text | raw | retrieval_query | code_query (legacy) | code_retrieval_query |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `content_embedding` (title+path) | summary | hit@10 .70 / MRR .574 | .72 / .581 | .70 / .585 | .68 / .568 |
| `content_embedding` (title+path) | content snippet | .80 / .665 | .78 / .627 | .67 / .484 | .72 / .527 |
| `content_embedding_768` (raw) | content snippet | .58 / .467 | **.63 / .548** | .43 / .299 | .43 / .291 |

Reading (n=60, roughly ±6 points; snippet queries are a content leak, so absolute numbers are optimistic): (1) the corpus/query recipe mismatch is NOT catastrophic on either column; (2) `retrieval_query` is never meaningfully worse than raw and is best on the raw ce768 corpus; (3) the `task: code retrieval` prefixes (`code_query`, `code_retrieval_query`) are clearly worse for content-like queries on both columns. **Recommended default for the adapter:** natural-language queries => `embedTextAs("retrieval_query", q)`; do not use `code_query` without its own evaluation; corpus recipes per column as recorded above. Caveat: an `ef_search`/HNSW approximation applies to the ce numbers; a larger, non-leaking labeled query set is still needed before any ranking claim (see RANK-EG-01).

### EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 — migration decision (2026-10-03): DEFERRED, evidence does not justify a blanket switch

- The 11 `embed.ts` importers query **different corpora and use the vector for different jobs**: `search-runtime.ts::makeEmbedFn` (feeds Qdrant dense retrieval across collections, and a 384-d projection variant), `context-assembler.ts` (query vectors for retrieval AND SOM BMU lookup `queryBmuCached` AND chunk-vs-query rerank via `embedTexts`), `evidence/search` (evidence_vectors corpus), `knowledge/stream`, `graph/recommendations`, `api/embed` (public embed endpoint), `ace-materializer`, `multihop-contextual-tree`, `attention-head-ranker`, `admin/batch-embeddings`. Changing the query prompt changes the vector space for SOM/BMU and non-code corpora whose recipes are unmeasured.
- The recall probe shows at most a ~+6 pt MRR gain (raw ce768 corpus only) and ~0 on the title-prefixed corpus. Not enough to take the regression risk without a per-corpus evaluation.
- **Policy until then:** existing callers keep plain `embedText`; **new** query paths over the code corpus use `embedTextAs("retrieval_query", q)`; never `code_query`/`code_retrieval_query` without an evaluation; any **document-side** write into `content_embedding` must use `embedTextAs("document", content, relative_path)`. Revisit per corpus via RANK-EG-01 (labeled, non-leaking query set).

### External review #2 reconciliation (retrieval-support architecture freeze) — 2026-10-03

**Adopted as invariants (add to the OpenSpec proposal, no code):** identity != hash; hash != centroid; centroid != semantic lane; executor != lane; Valkey != authority; SearXNG != retrieval authority; cache miss != canonical loss. Query path stays: query -> EmbeddingGemma -> nearest centroid(s) -> namespaced hash shortlist -> pgvector exact/HNSW -> optional canonical rerank -> ACE, with **fallback to full pgvector search whenever centroid filtering cannot prove acceptable recall** (the recall-shadow gate decides).

**Corrections to the pasted plan (checked against the repo):**
- **EMB-RECIPE-03 is already CLOSED** (this session; see its result section). The recipe is known per column, so the first blocker in the pasted chain is gone. The remaining blocker for the fast path is **CLUSTER-CENTROID-OWNER-01** (4 conflicting `gpu_cluster_centroids` writers).
- **EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 is NOT a prerequisite for centroid routing** and is deferred by evidence (recall probe: query-prefix choice is within noise; callers hit heterogeneous corpora). Centroid build/route only needs the *corpus* recipe, now fixed. It stays an optional later item; RANK-EG-01 gates any query-side change.
- **`CentroidArtifactV1` already exists** (`src/lib/server/atlas/cache/centroid-artifact-v1.ts`, strict Zod: `centroidId, workspaceRevision, representationRevision, clusteringRevision, dimensions, vectorChecksum, memberSetChecksum, memberCount, artifactRef, checksum`) together with a run-level `CentroidManifestV1` (`candidateSnapshotRevision, ordinalMapChecksum, clusteringPassId, algorithmRevision, parametersChecksum, candidateCount, ...`). The pasted run-level shape (`candidateSnapshotChecksum, ordinalMapChecksum, executorRevision, seed, k, centroidChecksum, assignmentChecksum`) is a **manifest** concern: do not add a second `CentroidArtifactV1`. HASHCENT-02 = extend `CentroidManifestV1` only for genuinely missing fields (`executorRevision`, `seed`, `k`, `assignmentChecksum`, `writesPerformed:false`) if `parametersChecksum` does not already cover seed/k, with its spec.
- **`CacheDescriptorV1` overlaps `AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1`** (`atlas/cache/ace-bitfrost-cache-identity-v1.ts`, which owns centroid/artifact/residency keys). HASHCENT-01 / CACHE-DESC-01 = add a `hashNamespace` and the `{namespace, lookupHash, centroidId, representationRevision, artifactChecksum}` descriptor *through that identity*, not a third key family. Namespaces stay disjoint (task block hash, report SHA-256, packet digest, normalized SearXNG-content hash); `packet_key` / `stableKey` remain identity. No vectors in Valkey.
- **Headroom:** the pasted `RuntimeHeadroomV1` (with `producerRevision`, `observedAt`, `maxAgeMs`, `canonicalAuthority:false`) is adopted as the contract for **HEADROOM-V2-01** (the id used in this file; the pasted `HEADROOM-01` is the same item). A sample older than `maxAgeMs` is treated as absent; it is a cost/residency feature only. It is distinct from the LangGraph Headroom V2 state budget (same word, different concern), so keep two ids.
- **New ids to add when scheduled (not yet in this file):** CACHE-DESC-01 (above); VALKEY-CANARY-01 (= HASHCENT-04: disposable write -> readback -> TTL expiry, operator-authorized, after the `maxmemory-policy`/TTL decision); TURBOVEC-HOT-01 (TurboVec as a hot challenger over centroid shortlists; challenger-only, no extra RRF vote); ACE-CACHE-ADMISSION-01 (admission of cache descriptors into ACE; blocked on the existing `NOT_SAFE_TO_PROJECT` verdict). **WEB-SEARX-01** keeps the chain external result -> normalize -> content SHA-256 -> dedupe -> provenance validation -> admission -> embed with the frozen recipe -> semantic index, retaining URL/title/snippet/fetch-time provenance; no canonical identity, no separate fusion vote.
- **HASHCENT-03 metrics (adopted):** Recall@10, Recall@50, p50, p95, **fallback rate**, **bucket size distribution**, versus whole-corpus exact pgvector (`atlas.exact_topk_oracle`); pass bar fixed before the run; negative results reported.

**Revised order:** ~~EMB-RECIPE-03~~ done -> semantic representation freeze (confirm `representationRevision` for `content_embedding` = title+path+trim and `content_embedding_768` = raw; pick the serving column) -> **CLUSTER-CENTROID-OWNER-01** -> HASHCENT-01 -> HASHCENT-02 -> HASHCENT-03 (shadow) -> HEADROOM-V2-01 -> CACHE-DESC-01 -> VALKEY-CANARY-01 -> WEB-SEARX-01 -> TURBOVEC-HOT-01 -> ACE-CACHE-ADMISSION-01. EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 is off the critical path.

### CLUSTER-CENTROID-OWNER-01 — read-only writer audit (2026-10-03; no code changed)

**Live table** `gpu_cluster_centroids`: columns `cluster_id integer (PK alone), cluster_type text, centroid_vec double[], chunk_count, topo_class, topo_byte, dominant_tags, purpose, metadata jsonb, updated_at`. Contents: **64 rows, all `kmeans_js`, all written 2026-07-14, 768-d**. No `representationRevision`, `workspaceRevision`, `clusteringRevision` or checksum column; the PK is `cluster_id` alone, so any writer using `ON CONFLICT (cluster_id)` silently overwrites another writer's row regardless of `cluster_type` or vector space.

| # | Writer | Input vectors | Behavior | Proposed label |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `scripts/atlas/persist-kmeans-centroids.mjs` | mean of `codebase_chunk_index.content_embedding` (title+path recipe) per `atlas_packets.topolog_method = 'phase3_kmeans_js'` cluster | **`TRUNCATE gpu_cluster_centroids`** then reinserts `cluster_type='kmeans_js'`; the only writer that matches the live rows | `COMPATIBILITY` (legacy JS-KMeans; destructive; produced the stale rows) |
| 2 | `sveltekit-frontend/scripts/sync-centroids-to-postgres.ts` | `embedded_summaries` (summary embeddings, a **different vector space**) via Redis | `ON CONFLICT (cluster_id) DO UPDATE` overwrites `cluster_type`/`centroid_vec` | `EXPERIMENT` / space-mixing hazard |
| 3 | `src/lib/server/retrieval/centroid-cache.ts` (persist path, reached from `api/codebase-index/centroids`) | Redis centroid JSON (`vector`, `topoClass`, `topoByte`) | Drizzle upsert on `cluster_id`, `chunkCount: 0`; overwrites type and vector | `BACKEND` of a cache; its PG persist step is the hazard |
| 4 | `scripts/atlas/dir-pipeline.mjs` | directory records | `INSERT … (cluster_id, centroid, label, source_count …)` — **those columns do not exist in the live table**, and `.catch(() => {})` hides the error, so it writes nothing | `DEAD` (schema-mismatched; archive, not delete) |

**Readers (all assume `kmeans_js`, 768-d, untracked revision):** `neo4j-cluster-fanout.mjs`, `project-valkey-centroid-prefill.mjs`, `prove-query-fanout-bitfrost-v1.mjs`, `warm-centroid-cache.mjs`, `centroid-cache.ts` (reads by `cluster_type`).

**Findings:** (1) there is no revision-qualified centroid set today; the 64 rows are 12 weeks old, JS-KMeans over atlas-packet clusters, and cannot back a recall claim. (2) Writers 2 and 3 can clobber writer 1's rows and mix summary-space vectors with chunk-space vectors under one PK. (3) Writer 4 is a no-op.

**Recommended owner design (needs operator confirmation before any code):** the new fast path must NOT use `gpu_cluster_centroids` as its source of truth. Owner = the existing `CentroidArtifactV1` + `CentroidManifestV1` contract (`atlas/cache/centroid-artifact-v1.ts`), produced by cuVS KMeans (`python/atlas_compute/cluster_softmax.py::run_cuvs_soft_kmeans`, WSL `atlas-rapids-cu13`) over the chosen corpus recipe, with `artifactRef` pointing at an artifact file (Arrow/mmap, not Postgres arrays). `gpu_cluster_centroids` stays a legacy `COMPATIBILITY` read surface; label it (add nothing to the schema without the Drizzle safety gate). Containment before any new writer exists (safe, small, not done yet): a ratchet spec that fails if a *new* file writes `gpu_cluster_centroids`, and a note in writers 2 and 3 that `ON CONFLICT (cluster_id)` is unsafe across types.

**Decisions needed from the operator:** (a) confirm the artifact-based owner and `gpu_cluster_centroids` = legacy read-only; (b) permission to add the writer-ratchet spec; (c) whether to archive writer 4 (`dir-pipeline.mjs` centroid block) and neutralize the destructive `TRUNCATE` in writer 1 behind an explicit flag.

- **Writer ratchet CREATED (2026-10-03, tests pass 3/3):** `src/lib/server/atlas/cache/gpu-cluster-centroids-writers.guard.spec.ts` freezes the 4 known writers of `gpu_cluster_centroids` (two-way: a new writer fails; a listed file that stops writing must be removed). Scans `scripts`, `python`, `packages`, `sveltekit-frontend/{scripts,src}` for INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE and Drizzle `.insert|update|delete(gpuClusterCentroids)`. No writer was changed, archived or neutralized; decisions (a) owner design, (c) archive `dir-pipeline.mjs` block / gate the `TRUNCATE` remain open for the operator.

### HEADROOM-V2-03 — admission-grade GPU memory slice: DONE, tests pass, live-proven (2026-10-03)

**Decision applied (operator guidance):** NVML/`nvidia-smi` path, not "measure CuPy once". Admission-grade sources = `nvml`, `nvidia-smi`. `cuda-runtime` and `rapids-sidecar-cupy` (`cudaMemGetInfo`-class) = diagnostic only, never sufficient for admission. CuPy sidecar was NOT started (GPU nearly full; low value).

**Built (no writes, no GPU workload, no CUDA context, no Valkey, no new sidecar, no new scheduler owner):**
- `gpu/gpu-residency-budget.ts` (the existing owner): `GpuTelemetrySource` gains `'nvidia-smi'`; `isAdmissionGradeGpuTelemetrySourceV1`; `GpuTelemetryFreshnessV1` gains opt-in `requireAdmissionGradeSource`; `planGpuResidencyV1` now rejects stale / future / unparseable / untrusted-source telemetry (reason text distinguishes them) and fails over to Qdrant with `telemetry: null`, `leaseableBytes: 0`. Defaults unchanged (no flag/window = old behaviour).
- `gpu/nvidia-smi-memory-client.ts` (new, the only new file in the chain): `createNvidiaSmiMemoryClient({exec?, now?, deviceIndex?}).readTelemetry()` runs `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.free,memory.total,memory.used,name --format=csv,noheader,nounits --id=N` as a separate OS process and emits the EXISTING `GpuMemoryTelemetryV1` with `source: 'nvidia-smi'`; timestamp taken after the read; any failure/garbage/inconsistent output => `null`.
- One caller wired: `routes/api/admin/atlas/synthesize/+server.ts` (the only production caller of `planGpuResidencyV1`) now reads ONLY the nvidia-smi producer (CuPy memory client removed from the admission path), `GPU_TELEMETRY_MAX_AGE_MS = 10_000`, `requireAdmissionGradeSource: true`. Route test mock updated.
- Ownership direction fixed: `orchestration/runtime-headroom-v1.ts` now imports the source-grade rule from the budget owner and composes via `buildRuntimeHeadroomV1FromGpuTelemetry` (GPU fields + source come from the telemetry; `observedAt` = oldest of GPU and host timestamps). `RuntimeHeadroomV1` no longer has its own GPU grade set.

**Tests (77 pass, 11 files, over `atlas/gpu`, `atlas/orchestration`, the synthesize route):** `nvidia-smi-memory-client.spec` 6; `gpu-telemetry-freshness-v1.spec` 13 (adds: age 9,999 ms usable / 10,001 ms absent / future absent; missing => Qdrant; nvml + nvidia-smi pass; cuda-runtime + CuPy rejected even when fresh with a "diagnostic only" reason; flag is opt-in; end to end nvidia-smi sample -> plan -> lease ALLOW, while 10,001 ms-old or exec failure => no ALLOW); `runtime-headroom-v1.spec` 9 (adds composer: GPU fields from telemetry, oldest-timestamp rule, CuPy-sourced telemetry yields no admission-grade VRAM); existing budget test 7 and route test 3 untouched in expectation.

**Live receipt (real module, real GPU, read-only):** `nvidia-smi` => free 1,098,907,648 B (1048 MiB; the tool's own query printed 1049 MiB), total 8,589,934,592 B, `source: nvidia-smi`, device RTX 3060 Ti. `planGpuResidencyV1` with the 10 s window + grade rule => `executionTarget: gpu`, `degraded: true` ("GPU pressure reduced bucket 512 -> 256"), `leaseableBytes: 830,472,192`, `maxCandidateBucket: 256`. (Free VRAM was 325 MiB earlier in the session and 1049 MiB now, so the reading tracks the changing real value; what the planner would have decided at 325 MiB was not run.)

**Not done:** only the synthesize route is wired; the other consumers below are not. No sampler loop/cache: each request reads nvidia-smi once (a process spawn per request; acceptable for an admin route, measure before wiring a hot path).

### Report-size gate: where the "10 MB" actually lives (2026-10-03, read-only audit; nothing changed except two CLAUDE.md corrections)

- **No active hook enforces 10 MB.** `core.hooksPath` is `.git/hooks` (post-checkout, post-commit, post-merge, pre-push, all Git LFS shims; no pre-commit). The repo ships `.githooks/pre-commit`, which blocks files **> 20 MB** (20,971,520 B) and common binary extensions, exempts `filter=lfs` paths, and is **not active** unless `core.hooksPath` is pointed at `.githooks` (enabling it is a repo-wide operator decision: the next regenerated report over 20 MB would be rejected).
- **The "10 MB gate" is advisory:** `scripts/atlas/audit-openspec-report-disposition-v1.mjs` (`sizeLimitBytes = 10_000_000`, counts `over10MB`). It is a report-disposition audit, not a git gate. The other session's "over the 10 MB gate ... stayed in %TEMP%" meant it chose not to overwrite the shared report; `%TEMP%` is outside the repo (the repo's gitignored `.tmp/` is a different directory).
- **The tree already violates it:** at `HEAD`, `docs/reports/` holds many tracked files over 10 MB (largest: `source-ref-binding-v1.json` 44.5 MB, `graphify-workspace-snapshot-binding-v1.json` 42.8 MB, `openspec-file-kmeans-v1.json` 33.8 MB, `temporal-event-candidates-v1.json` 31.5 MB, `structured-lexical-fanout.json` 25.8 MB, ...). `openspec-workboard-v1.json` is 15.1 MB at `HEAD` (not LFS: `filter` unspecified) and 42.5 MB in the working tree. GitHub itself warns at 50 MB and rejects at 100 MB per file (general knowledge, not tested here), so a 45 MB workboard is under the hard limit but close to the warning, and every regeneration stores a whole new blob in history.
- **Corrected CLAUDE.md:** two statements claimed "pre-commit hook rejects >10MB" / "files over 10 MB cannot enter git (hook)"; both now describe the real behaviour above.
- **Recommended handling (becomes WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01; not implemented):** stop committing the full workboard ledger. Commit a small manifest (schema, generatedAt, total bytes, SHA-256, task counts, per-change shard list) plus shards split per change/lane each well under 10 MB, and keep the full ledger either in gitignored `.tmp/` or under a narrowly path-scoped LFS rule (`.gitattributes` already carries two such path rules). Before regenerating the workboard, decide this; otherwise the next run replaces a 15 MB tracked blob with a 45 MB one. Same treatment applies to the other >20 MB reports listed above (their own follow-up, one report at a time).
- **Operator decisions:** (a) manifest + shards vs LFS vs `.tmp` for the workboard; (b) whether to activate `.githooks` (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) given the existing >20 MB tracked reports.

### Open-task ranking, page 1 of 728 (2026-10-03; read-only; ledger `generatedAt` 2026-09-26)

**Funnel (from `docs/reports/openspec-workboard-v1.json`):** 3,195 open (2,431 ACTIONABLE + 764 WAITING_ON_DEPENDENCY) -> 2,431 actionable -> 2,017 `gateState: READY` and `controllerState: ACTIONABLE` -> 1,241 with `mutationClass` CODE_ONLY or READ_ONLY (no DB / DDL / cache / projection / Graphify writes) -> **728 still unchecked in their tasks.md today** (513 were closed or moved since the ledger was built) across 53 changes.

**Method:** `priority` ascending (it is the builder's own keyword heuristic `priorityFor(change + text)`, P10 most urgent; 190 tasks sit at P10), then overlap with the requested themes (nlp / centroid / valkey / workers / simdjson / graph / knn-topk-ordinal), then one task per change per round for diversity, then `stableKey`. Keyset-style pagination by offset over that fixed order.

**Not used, with reasons:** PageRank (only 3 of 9,619 tasks have any dependency edge, and `criticalPathRank`, `fanoutCount`, `writeRisk`, `proofLevel` are null for all 2,431 actionable tasks, so the graph is degenerate); centroids / kNN over task text (no task-card embeddings or task ordinal map exist yet: TASKCARD-INDEX-01, WB-ORDINAL-MAP-01); Valkey, RabbitMQ, CPU worker pool, simdjson (not needed to rank 3k rows; the 42 MB ledger parses in seconds). TRACE `openspec_frontier` returns the first unproven claims alphabetically by change (confidence 0, `totalUnproven` 11,022), so it is not a ranking; `openspec_census` is `UNAVAILABLE`.

Check `retrieval/candidate-manifest-v1.ts` (existing cursor handling) before writing a new cursor contract. |
| 10 | **TASK-GRAPH / PAGERANK-01** | NetworkX oracle first, cuGraph challenger after. **Caveat measured today:** the ledger has explicit dependency edges on only 3 of 9,619 tasks; 408 of 3,195 open tasks contain a dependency phrase (after / requires / blocked by / ...), but only 62 of those cite a task-like ID. So PageRank / `unblocksCount` signals stay weak until dependency edges are materialized: first an NLP dependency-phrase pass that emits CANDIDATE edges (never authority), then reviewed edges. |

**Where the other ideas fit (adopted):**
- **NLP passes** are feature producers into the matrix, never task identity or proof state. First columns: `domainId, actionClass, artifactType, requiresRuntime, requiresWrite, hasEvidence, hasReceipt, dependencyCount, blockedByCount`; later `semanticScore, domainAffinity, historicalSuccess`.
- **CPU workers first:** bounded worker pool for TaskCard shard parsing, NLP extraction, dependency-edge materialization, checksums, the NetworkX oracle and the exact top-k baseline. **No new RabbitMQ consumer for this** (it would create a second orchestration owner); use the existing queue/lease owner.
- **simdjson** only after the manifest/shard format is frozen, for ingest of the audit JSON / JSONL shards; never replacing Zod, TypeScript or Pydantic validation.
- **Valkey centroids, cuVS KMeans, cuGraph, SIMT / cuTile, BitFrost, TurboVec** come after the deterministic matrix and exact oracle; KMeans gives routing locality, never task priority; Valkey holds only `{taskOrdinal, taskRevision, centroidId, representationRevision, artifactChecksum}`.
- **`CandidateFeatureMatrixV1` first columns:** identity/state (`ordinal, claimState, proofState, lifecycleState`), dependency (`dependencyCount, blockedByCount, unblocksCount, dependencyDepth`), evidence (`evidenceCount, receiptCount, hasRevisionBoundReceipt, proofCoverage`), risk (`writeRisk, externalMutationRequired, operatorApprovalRequired`), structural (`pagerank, ppr`).
- **Initial score, no training:** `proofGapWeight + unblocksCount + pagerank + currentGoalAffinity - writeRisk - operatorApprovalPenalty - unresolvedOwnerPenalty`, with a per-result explanation (e.g. "blocks semantic lane authority; 2 live registries conflict; read-only/code-only"). A tournament (deterministic incumbent vs semantic vs PageRank/PPR vs NLP vs learned) comes after the first top-10 works.

**Adopted spine:** WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 -> WB-STATE / WB-CARD -> task `CandidateOrdinalMap` -> deterministic `CandidateFeatureMatrix` -> dependency graph + NetworkX oracle -> semantic TaskCard index -> top-10 + cursor pagination -> PageRank/PPR features -> tournament -> cuGraph / cuVS -> Valkey / BitFrost hot projections. The three MCP gates run in parallel. Graphify daily, SeaweedFS writes, Valkey writes and broad GPU jobs stay deferred until a selected proof needs them.

**Review #5 adopted (ranker hygiene):** the keyword ranking stays advisory until it is bound to a fresh Workboard revision. Promote it to `scripts/atlas/rank-open-tasks-v1.mjs` (**RANK-OPEN-TASKS-01**) with: input = Workboard manifest/revision + current tasks.md files; filters = actionable, gate-ready, CODE_ONLY | READ_ONLY, currently unchecked (waiting tasks only on request, write-gated excluded by default); score = declared priority, gate readiness, mutation class, theme affinity; output = rank, stableKey, logicalTaskKey, changeId, source line, score breakdown, workboardRevision, sourceRevision, currentLineVerified. **One task per change is an option (`--diversify-by-change`), not part of the canonical order** (two real blockers from one change must be able to appear together). Deterministic tests: same input same order; closed task excluded; moved task re-resolved or rejected; waiting excluded unless requested; write-gated excluded; ties by stableKey; stale revision warns / fails closed; pagination has no duplicates or skips. Do not implement DIR-INDEX-07E from the week-old page; rerank on the fresh snapshot first and implement it only if it stays near the top and is on the current critical path.

**Priority now (intersection of heuristic rank, architectural blocker, read-only/code-only, no owner conflict):** WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 -> RANK-OPEN-TASKS-01 -> rerank on fresh snapshot -> MCP-LANE-OWNER-01 -> MCP-LANE-VOTE-01 -> MCP-LIVE-PARITY-01 -> WB-STATE / WB-CARD (reconciled with EvidenceCard) -> task CandidateOrdinalMap -> deterministic CandidateFeatureMatrix -> TASKCARD-INDEX / top-k pagination. The richer stack (NLP features, KNN, PageRank/PPR, tournament, cuVS / cuGraph, Valkey / BitFrost) starts only after those.

**To "run analysis on the OpenSpecs with GPU / NLP / pgvector":** the real blockers are (1) a model-artifact receipt for the embedding model (`modelId`, `modelArtifactRevision`), (2) the projection-parity gate (exact pgvector oracle + `semantic_768` truth set + candidate ordinal map), then (3) live cuVS / cuGraph. Run the two named read-only plan scripts first; do not embed the 55,610 inputs or start a GPU job until the receipt exists.

### External review #6 reconciliation: task evidence graph, hypergraph, tournament, predictive stack (2026-10-03)

**Verdict: adopted as the design for the richer stack, with ordering and ownership corrections. No code was written for it.** The reviewer's central point matches the measurement already recorded: PageRank is premature on the explicit dependency graph (3 of 9,619 tasks have an edge; 62 of 408 dependency-phrase tasks cite a task-like ID), so PageRank/PPR waits until a provenance-classed evidence graph exists.

**Existence check (git grep, 2026-10-03):** `TaskGraphEdgeV1`, `TaskHyperedgeV1`, `TaskNlpFactV1`, `TournamentResultV1`, `RecoveryAnnotationV1` have zero hits in tracked source: all five are new names. `CandidateFeatureMatrix` / `CandidateOrdinalMap` already exist (reuse, task-universe ordinal space). `dag_hit` exists only in lineage gates and audits, not as a recovery cache.

| Pasted gate | Mapped to | My correction |
|---|---|---|
| GATE 1 WB-CARD-01 | existing WB-CARD-01 | Extend the EvidenceCard fabric (11,122 cards) first; but WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 stays ahead because regeneration blocks on report size. |
| GATE 2 WB-ORDINAL-MAP-01 | existing | Own `ordinalMapChecksum`; do not share code-chunk ordinal space. |
| GATE 3 TASK-NLP-01 (`TaskNlpFactV1`) | new id | Producer = `:8095` sidecar via the existing `evidence-entity-extractors.ts`; facts carry source offsets and `canonicalAuthority:false`. Never a task identity or proof state. |
| GATE 4 TASK-GRAPH-01 (`TaskGraphEdgeV1`) | new id | Edge classes by provenance: DECLARED / SOURCE_EVIDENCE / RECEIPT are structural; NLP_GROUNDED / SEMANTIC_PROPOSAL are proposals only. First pass emits candidate edges from the 408 dependency-phrase tasks. |
| GATE 5 TASK-HYPERGRAPH-01 (`TaskHyperedgeV1`) | new id | Check `hyperrag-packet-rpc.ts` and the hypergraph specs for an existing edge contract before defining a second; pairwise projection must be deterministic (CSR/COO) and carry `graphRevision`. |
| GATE 6 TASK-GRAPH-ORACLE-01 | existing NetworkX/cuGraph parity pipeline + `GraphExecutionSemanticsV1` | Reuse; no new graph-algorithm dispatcher. |
| GATE 7 TASKCARD-INDEX-01 | existing | `embedTextAs('document', ...)`, separate namespace; blocked by the embedding model-artifact receipt (`MODEL_ARTIFACT_UNPROVEN`). |
| GATE 8 TASK-TOPK-01 | existing TOP-K item | Check `retrieval/candidate-manifest-v1.ts` cursor handling first. |
| GATE 9 WB-FEATURE-MATRIX-01 | existing | Deterministic columns first. |
| GATE 10 WB-TOURNAMENT-01 | new id | Simulation only: `writesPerformed:false`, `tasksMdChanged:false`, zero Postgres / Valkey / Qdrant / graph writes. Receipts as JSONL (`TournamentResultV1`: candidateOrdinal, round, scorerId, scorerRevision, score, disposition ADVANCE / ELIMINATED / INVALID, reason). Losers are never deleted. Pattern precedent: `som_cache_tournament_01.py` (CPU, fixed trace). |

**Corrections and cautions**
- **The tournament is a ranking evaluator, not a learner.** Round order (deterministic incumbent -> semantic -> graph -> learned low-rank) is sound, but every challenger must score the same frozen cohort, and round sizes (147/64/32/16/8) are illustrative, not tuned.
- **AlphaEvolve framing is an analogy only.** Nothing like it exists in the repo. Do not build candidate mutation or RL/PPO until deterministic replay, a stable cohort and outcome receipts exist. XGBoost as first supervised challenger is reasonable, but there are no outcome receipts today, so it cannot be trained yet.
- **Semantic votes:** pgvector, Qdrant, cuVS and TurboVec are executors under one `semantic_768` lane, one vote (ties into MCP-LANE-VOTE-01).
- **okf:** use the existing okf loader paths (`ace/code-intel-service.ts`, `acp/packet-assembler.ts`); do not create a new `okf/` schema directory or a sixth ontology tuple store.
- **Sections 8-9 (`RecoveryAnnotationV1`, MessagePack BYTEA cache)** concern the error-recovery / DAG-hit design, not task ranking. Recorded as a separate, unscheduled idea: annotations stay out of the canonical packet schema, the BYTEA blob is a replayable derived artifact, and queryable fields stay in normal columns. No work until a recovery owner is identified.
- **Arrow/tensor materialization** only after the graph and matrix semantics are validated; JSON stays the audit format. simdjson stays after the shard format is frozen.
- **PCA/SVD/KMeans/UMAP roles** match the census (UMAP has no implementation; visualization only, never routing).

**Order unchanged:** WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 -> RANK-OPEN-TASKS-01 -> fresh rerank -> MCP gates (parallel) -> WB-STATE/WB-CARD -> ordinal map -> TASK-NLP-01 -> TASK-GRAPH-01 (+ hyperedges) -> NetworkX oracle -> feature matrix -> TASKCARD-INDEX -> top-k -> tournament (simulation) -> cuGraph/cuVS -> supervised challenger only after outcome receipts exist.

### OPEN-SPEC-DB-AUDIT-01 (read-only, 2026-10-03) + review #7 reconciliation
Method: read the four manual migrations; `git grep` for writers/readers across `.mjs/.ts/.mts/.py`. **Finding: zero INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE writers exist for any of these objects.** Only the plan/audit scripts `plan-openspec-evidence-ledger-import-v1.mjs`, `audit-openspec-evidence-ledger-readback-v1.mjs`, `audit-openspec-evidence-migration-v1.mjs` (and `plan-openspec-evidence-retrieval-v1.mjs` for chunks) mention them. Apply status of all: NOT APPLIED (live DB has none; dry-run `sqlApplied:false`).

| Object | Role | Class | Identity / revision / lineage | Mutability |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| openspec_changes | change metadata | PROJECTION of `openspec/changes/*` | `change_id`; `workspace_revision`; `tasks_hash` | upsert by import |
| openspec_tasks | task identity + current claim | PROJECTION of tasks.md (tasks.md stays claim authority) | `canonical_task_key`, `(change_id,task_id)`, `task_ref`; `current_revision`; `task_hash` | upsert; lifecycle_state moves |
| openspec_task_revisions | claim history | HISTORY | `(task_row_id,revision)`; `claim_hash`; `observation_checksum` | append-only (trigger rejects UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE) |
| openspec_task_aliases | renamed/moved ids | HISTORY | `alias_key`; `checksum` | append-only (trigger) |
| openspec_dependencies | explicit task edges | PROJECTION (declared edges only) | composite PK incl. `edge_type`; `source_ref` | upsert |
| evidence_receipts | validation receipts | CANONICAL for receipts only once imported (docs/reports JSON stays authority until applied + readback-proven) | `evidence_id`; `checksum` unique; `workspace_revision`; `task_revision` | immutable by convention (no trigger) |
| task_evidence | task <-> receipt link | PROJECTION | composite PK | upsert |
| openspec_task_predicate | per-task predicates | DERIVED | `predicate_id`, `checksum`, revisions | replace-by-revision |
| evidence_assertion | per-receipt assertions | DERIVED from receipt `assertions` jsonb | `(evidence_id,assertion_id)` | replace-by-receipt |
| openspec_supersession | change supersession | PROJECTION (declared) | `supersession_id`, `checksum` | upsert |
| openspec_evidence_chunks | text + vector(768) | DERIVED semantic projection (needs the embedding model-artifact receipt) | `chunk_id`, `checksum`, `representation_revision='semantic_768'` | rebuildable |
| openspec_task_current / _v2 | current proof state | DERIVED_CURRENT_VIEW (v2 requires passing assertions + readback; supersedes v1 view) | n/a | view |
| task_evidence_binding | explicit-name view over task_evidence | DERIVED_CURRENT_VIEW | n/a | view |

**Authority-duplication answer:** not a duplicate of `analysis_pass_results` / `analysis_pass_current` (code-analysis pass ledger, different grain) nor of `atlas_openspec_board_snapshots` (summary history only). Real overlaps to manage: (1) `openspec_tasks` vs tasks.md and the Workboard ledger (tasks.md stays authoritative for claims; Postgres is a revision-bound projection; the Workboard `executionState` stays out of these tables); (2) `evidence_receipts` vs EvidenceCards (11,122 cards; cards are the compiled packets, receipts the validation records, join on `change_id`+`task_id`+revision). `openspec_task_current` (v1) and `_v2` coexist; treat v2 as the reader and v1 as LEGACY once v2 is applied. Open before apply: no `ON CONFLICT` import writer exists, receipts have no immutability trigger, `evidence_receipts` FKs the revisions table only when both columns are set.

**Review #7 reconciliation**
- Adopted: Track A (Workboard) and Track B (persistent evidence model) run independently; classification before declarations (above); parity gate (`OPEN-SPEC-DB-SCHEMA-PARITY-01` = `openspec-evidence-schema.spec.ts`, 14 tests passing); read-only Studio only; no apply, import, embed, Valkey, BitFrost or Graphify-daily coupling yet; the import -> receipt -> embed -> pgvector oracle -> ordinal map -> parity -> ranking order.
- **Disagree on one point:** the review wants the declarations "part of the configured schema owner" (`tablesFilter`). `tablesFilter` only suppresses DROPs for DB-only tables; it does not suppress CREATE for tables declared in the schema but missing from the DB (Drizzle Safety Rule). Registering these 11 unapplied tables in `schema.ts` would make `drizzle-kit generate` emit CREATEs that collide with the manual SQL. They stay unregistered (spec-enforced) until apply, then register in the same change as the apply.
- Studio: the read-only page already exists at `/atlas/studio/openspec`; surfacing the blocker states (`MODEL_ARTIFACT_UNPROVEN`, `PROJECTION_PARITY_UNPROVEN`, `RRF_RUNTIME_UNEXECUTED`, `MULTIHOP_LINEAGE_UNPROVEN`) as neutral status chips there is the remaining OPEN-SPEC-STUDIO-01 work, not yet done.

### WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 built + review #8 (ANALYSIS-* subtasks) reconciliation (2026-10-03)

**WB-REPORT-MANIFEST-01 (code + dry run done).** `scripts/atlas/build-workboard-report-manifest-v1.mjs` (+ `.test.mjs`, 7 node:test tests passing). Splits `docs/reports/openspec-workboard-v1.json` (42,466,502 B, CRLF) into 14 shards under gitignored `.tmp/atlas/workboard-shards/` (`core.json`, six section JSONs, seven `taskInventory.NNNN.jsonl` row shards, max 4,194,143 B, none over 10 MB) plus `manifest.json` (raw + canonical SHA-256, per-shard SHA-256/bytes/rowCount, `keyOrder`, `canonicalAuthority:false`). The round trip is verified against the canonical LF serialization (the source file is CRLF, so a raw-byte match is impossible; both hashes are recorded). Tampered or missing shards fail closed. The script writes only under `--out`; nothing in `docs/reports` was touched. **Not done:** committing a manifest copy under `docs/reports/` (decision pending), regenerating the ledger itself, and wiring the builder to emit shards directly.

### ANALYSIS-EVIDENCECARD-OWNER-01 (read-only reconciliation, 2026-10-03)
**Correction to earlier counts:** the generated corpus `docs/reports/openspec-evidence-cards-v1.json` holds **11,024** cards (not 11,122; that figure came from a different report). Proof states: CLAIM_ONLY 10,893 / BLOCKED 89 / STALE 39 / PARTIAL 1 / PROVEN 2; `proofUsableCount` 0. Identity: DECLARED_ID 3,755 / DERIVED_ID 6,904 / AMBIGUOUS 365. Only 39 cards carry dependency refs and 4 carry receipt refs. Workboard parity (`WORKBOARD_PARITY_NOT_PROVEN`): 9,619 board tasks vs 11,024 cards, 8,180 exact source-line joins, 337 board-only, 1,742 evidence-only.

**Three card-like shapes exist today:**
1. **Generated card** `atlas.openspec-evidence-card.v1`, emitted by `scripts/atlas/compile-openspec-evidence-cards-v1.mjs`: `cardId, taskIdentity{taskRef,taskId,changeId,canonicalTaskRef,authorityScope,identityState}, claim, predicates[], proofState, proofAuthority, blockers, contradictions, dependencyRefs, receiptRefs, sourceRefs, revisions{workspaceRevision,sourceRevision}, contextBlob, tokenEstimate/Budget, retrievalUsable, proofUsable, checksum`. This is the real 11,024-card corpus.
2. **Contract card** `atlas.evidence-card.v1` (`EvidenceCardV1Schema`, `packages/semantic-contracts/src/openspec-evidence-fabric-v1.ts`): a different, flatter field set (`taskRef, changeId, taskId, claim, proofState, sourceRef, conceptID, confidenceScore, evidenceIds, workspaceRevision`). **The compiler does not use `buildEvidenceCardV1`**; only `audit-openspec-evidence-fabric-v1.mjs` and rerank-shadow harnesses import the contract. So the typed contract and the generated corpus have diverged (two schema strings for one concept).
3. **Board task** `OpenSpecBoardTaskV1` (`openspec-board/types.ts`, UI read model) and the Workboard `taskInventory` row (`executionState, gateState, mutationClass, priority, lane, stableKey`). Plus the unrelated retrieval `candidate-evidence-card-v1/v2` family (candidate cards, not task cards; do not merge).

**Proposed decision (for operator confirmation; nothing implemented):**
- The generated card (1) is the evidence unit and the owner. `OpenSpecTaskCardV1` is NOT a new card: it is a derived projection `card + Workboard row`, joined on the card's `taskIdentity.taskRef` (= `source#Lline`) with the Workboard `source`+`line`, adding only `executionState, gateState, mutationClass, priority, lane, stableKey, workboardRevision` and the derived `writeRisk`/`proofLevel`.
- Resolve the schema-string split before any code: either (a) retire the contract card's flat shape in favor of the generated one (migrate the audit script and harnesses), or (b) make the compiler emit through the contract. (a) is smaller: 2 audit/harness consumers vs the corpus compiler. Needs your choice.
- The 337 board-only and 1,742 evidence-only rows must be classified (moved lines, new tasks since the ledger, archived changes) before the projection can claim a join; unmatched rows stay explicit, never dropped.
- Blocker status to expose: `TASKCARD_EVIDENCECARD_OWNER_UNRESOLVED` until (a)/(b) is chosen.

`scripts/atlas/rank-open-tasks-v1.mjs` + `.test.mjs` (9 node:test tests passing: determinism, closed excluded, moved re-resolved when unique / rejected when ambiguous or gone, waiting and write-gated excluded by default, ties by stableKey, optional `--diversify-by-change`, drift-safe pagination via `orderingChecksum`, advisory/no-writes flags). ### MCP-LANE-OWNER-01 (read-only, 2026-10-03): largely already resolved; corrected a stale pointer
- **The v1/v2 "conflict" was resolved on 2026-09-27** (`openspec/changes/parent-atlas-agentic-file-compiler/tasks.md`, "Concurrent-write collision"): `helper-registry-v1.ts` and its `*-v1` set are the CANONICAL implementation; the `*-v2` set (built first in a different session, renamed only because a concurrent write landed on the v1 path) is a comparison copy, "not a second runtime owner". My earlier review-#3 note and `claude.md` called v2 "the registry"; both were wrong. `claude.md` now points at v1. Label: v1 = CANONICAL_OWNER, v2 = COMPATIBILITY (comparison; do not extend).
- Outside `agentic-file-compiler/` the only consumer of either chain is `scripts/atlas/afc-helper-eligibility-live-proof-v1.mjs`; there is no production route using them, so the disagreement had no runtime effect.
- **Remaining real finding (executor != lane):** v1 `semantic-768` owner = `retrieval/semantic-search-workflow.ts` (query -> candidates; the helper/lane). v2 listed `embedding/canonical-embed.ts:embedSemantic768Canonical`, which only produces a vector (executor `llama-server /v1/embeddings`, no search). `embedSemantic768Canonical` is a strict executor that already REQUIRES `model`, `modelArtifactRevision`, `tokenizerRevision`, `inputPolicyRevision` and allows no Ollama/ONNX/zero-vector fallback; callers: `ace/context-assembler.ts:5532`, `research/web-research-ingester.ts`. So the v1 assignment is right (lane owner) and `embedSemantic768Canonical` is the embedding EXECUTOR beneath it.
- **Useful for ANALYSIS-MODEL-RECEIPT-01:** the `Semantic768CanonicalResult` shape (`model`, `modelArtifactRevision`, `tokenizerRevision`, `inputPolicyRevision`, `admittedTokenCount`) is the receipt vocabulary the OpenSpec embedding plan needs (`modelId`, `modelArtifactRevision` currently null). Reuse it; do not invent a second receipt shape. The artifact checksum is the one field it does not carry.
- Still open: MCP-LANE-VOTE-01 (one vote per logical lane across helper / MCP / executor roles) and MCP-LIVE-PARITY-01 (static registry vs live TRACE `tools/list`); neither started.

### ANALYSIS-MODEL-RECEIPT-01 (2026-10-03): plan now binds to the existing EMB-PROV-01 receipt
- **Found, not built:** `docs/reports/emb-prov-01-embedding-provenance-receipt.json` (2026-09-27, `EMB_PROV_01_PROVEN`) already proves the artifact: `models/embeddinggemma-300m-f16.gguf`, SHA-256 `bc843658e96d2e9cc7c3402332b158f0cc4f73e61b23cef9a41acee1c0d372b7` matching the recorded `EMBEDDING_MODEL_ARTIFACT_REVISION` and `EMBEDDING_GGUF_SHA256`, and llama-server `:8081` vs Ollama `:11434` agree (768-d, both L2 = 1, cosine 0.999988, threshold 0.999). The OpenSpec embedding plan simply never read it (it took `modelId`/`modelArtifactRevision` from env vars only, hence `MODEL_ARTIFACT_UNPROVEN`).
- **Change:** `scripts/atlas/plan-openspec-evidence-embeddings-v1.mjs` gained exported `resolveModelBinding` (+ `plan-openspec-evidence-embeddings-v1.test.mjs`, 5 tests passing) and a main-guard. The model binds only from a receipt that is PROVEN, checksum-matched (both vars), sha256-shaped, executors agreeing, 768-d and fresh (`OPENSPEC_MODEL_RECEIPT_MAX_AGE_HOURS`, default 168). Env strings alone are reported as `ENV_OVERRIDE`, never proven. `modelId` is derived from the artifact filename (`embeddinggemma-300m-f16`) and flagged as derived.
- **Result (written to `.tmp/atlas/embedding-plan-receipt-bound.json` only; the tracked `docs/reports` plan was NOT regenerated):** status `INPUTS_READY_NO_EMBEDDINGS`, model bound, **plannedInputs 55,120 = 11,024 cards x 5** (earlier 55,610 / 11,122 figures were from a different report).
- **Caveats:** (1) the receipt is ~160 h old, so it ages out of the 168 h window within hours and the status reverts to `MODEL_ARTIFACT_UNPROVEN` until the receipt is regenerated; (2) the receipt proves local artifact identity then, not that an executor is up now: `:8081` did not answer on 2026-10-03 and Ollama was not re-probed; (3) it carries no `tokenizerRevision` / `inputPolicyRevision`, which `embedSemantic768Canonical` requires per call; (4) the origin of the GGUF is unconfirmed (an earlier session found a checksum mismatch against the cited ggml-org release), so this proves local identity, not upstream provenance; (5) the stored 55k corpus vectors came from Ollama `embeddinggemma:latest`, whose parity with the GGUF (cosine 0.99999) is what makes the two interchangeable, not a bit-identical guarantee.
- **Still gated:** no embedding was run, nothing written to Postgres/Qdrant. Next gates are the pgvector exact oracle and semantic_768 truth set (ANALYSIS-PGVECTOR-ORACLE-01, SEMANTIC768-TRUTHSET-01), then projection parity.

### ANALYSIS-PGVECTOR-ORACLE-01 / SEMANTIC768-TRUTHSET-01 / PROJECTION-PARITY-01 (read-only audit, 2026-10-03): method already proven on a code cohort; OpenSpec corpus has no vectors
- **Nothing to build for the oracle method.** An exact pgvector oracle, a Qdrant executor comparison and a cuVS exact triple-parity already exist for the bounded PKT-LINEAGE-08A code cohort: `scripts/atlas/prove-retrieval-01l-08a-semantic-query-parity-v1.mjs` (Postgres `<=>` exact vs Qdrant `has_id`-restricted; frozen query fixture with `queryEmbeddingChecksum`; reports `EXACT_SET_PARITY`, recall@10 = 1, rank parity true, 434/434 projection parity), `scripts/atlas/export-retrieval-01l-08a-cuvs-fixture-v1.mjs` + `python/atlas_compute/gpu_mini_fabric/retrieval_01l_08a_cuvs_exact_v1.py` (`CUVS_POSTGRES_CPU_TRIPLE_EXACT_PARITY`). These are LANE != EXECUTOR proofs (one vote). The 2026-09-05 results are for the code cohort, not OpenSpec, and are not evidence for the OpenSpec corpus.
- **The OpenSpec SQL oracle is designed, not runnable:** `openspec-evidence-retrieval-plan-v1.json` carries an exact `ORDER BY embedding <=> $1, chunk_id` query over `openspec_evidence_chunks` joined to `openspec_task_current`, `liveQueryExecuted:false`. It cannot run: that table does not exist live and no OpenSpec card has an embedding.
- **Blocking chain for the OpenSpec corpus (each step needs explicit approval):** apply migrations (phase 4) -> ledger import -> embed 55,120 inputs through the receipt-bound model -> run the oracle -> freeze the truth set -> cuVS exact parity. The model receipt gate is now closed (see above); the others are not.
- **A DB-free bounded alternative (proposal, not started):** embed a small frozen cohort of cards (e.g. 200) through the receipt-bound executor into a `.tmp` fixture, then reuse the 08A fixture-export + cuVS exact harness against it, with a CPU exact reference. It needs the embedding executor up (`:8081` was down) and a small GPU slice, so it needs operator OK; it would exercise the method on OpenSpec text but would not replace the Postgres oracle.
- **Truth set (SEMANTIC768-TRUTHSET-01):** needs reviewed relevance judgments; none exist for OpenSpec cards. A defensible first set is structural, not judged: for cards that cite a declared task id or a change, "neighbors" = same-change cards, scored as a weak-label recall check only, never as relevance ground truth.
