### MCP-LANE-VOTE-01 (guard form, 2026-10-03): vote arithmetic already exists; added a registry-to-vote freeze
- **Existing owners (no new fusion owner added):** vote arithmetic = `retrieval/rrf-combiner.ts` (`combineViaRRF`: best score per logical lane per hit, executors kept as `laneSupport`/breakdown provenance); alias vocabulary = `retrieval/retrieval-lane-aliases.ts` (`normalizeRetrievalLane`, dense + lexical only); reports `rrf-logical-lane-alias-map-v1.json` (`ALIAS_MAP_PARTIAL_MIGRATION_BLOCKED`, 2026-09-10: `turbovec`, `postgres_trigram` flagged EXECUTOR_NAME_NOT_LANE), `rrf-caller-baseline-v1.json` (`CALLER_BASELINE_INCOMPLETE_MIGRATION_BLOCKED`, 93 callers), `rf6-executor-lane-owner-v1.json` (`RF6_EXECUTOR_LANE_OWNER_PROVEN_SOURCE_TRACE`).
- **Added:** `src/lib/server/atlas/agentic-file-compiler/helper-lane-vote-map.guard.spec.ts` (7 tests passing). Freezes a row for every one of the 12 canonical (v1) registry helpers: VOTING_EXECUTOR (`rg-exact`->rg, `postgres-fts` + `postgres-trigram`->lexical, `semantic-768`->dense, `ast-grep-structural`->ast), EVIDENCE (`tree-sitter-chunk`, `ts-morph-symbol`, `lsp-definition`, `lsp-references`, `langextract-grounding`), FEATURE (`docs-corpus-search`, `graph-ppr`). A new registry helper with no row fails the spec. Also proves in the real combiner that qdrant + turbovec + go-semantic collapse to ONE semantic vote while a candidate seen by two different logical lanes gets two.
- **Real gaps found (documented, NOT fixed):** (1) `rrf-combiner.ts` knows only `qdrant_vector / go_retrieval_semantic / turbovec_ann` as semantic executors (logical name `'semantic'`), while `retrieval-lane-aliases.ts` calls the same lane `'dense'` and also lists `qdrant`, `qdrant_768`, `cuvs`, `cagra`; the two vocabularies disagree on the lane name. (2) A `cuvs`/`cagra` name fed to `combineViaRRF` today casts a SECOND vote (asserted by the KNOWN GAP test, which must be updated when the combiner is widened). (3) `RetrievalLaneName` has no `lexical`/`rg`/`exact` members, so the combiner cannot express the helper lanes above except `postgres_trigram`. Fixing these belongs to the existing RF7 convergence work (`FusionContributionV1`), not a new owner; relevant before cuVS ever feeds fusion.
- **Not done from the review's list:** a runtime Zod parse asserting `canonicalAuthority:false` on helper/MCP/executor outputs (the guard is structural only); MCP tools and diagnostic MCP lanes (`atlas_exact_topk_oracle`, `atlas_packet_dense_search`, ...) are not yet classified against lanes. That classification is MCP-LIVE-PARITY-01's input.

### MCP-LIVE-PARITY-01 (2026-10-03): reused the existing census reconcile; static-vs-live PASS, lane classification still open
- **Ran** `npm run trace:mcp:census-reconcile` (`sveltekit-frontend/scripts/trace-mcp-census-reconcile-01.mjs`, read-only against the live server; no new census written). Server `HEALTHY`. Static `registerTool()` scan of `trace-mcp-server.ts`: **120**; live `tools/list` at `:8788/mcp`: **188**; shared 120, static-only 0, runtime-only 68 (all classified `DELEGATED_MODULE_REGISTRATION` or compatibility alias), **duplicate runtime names 0, unexplained 0, verdict PASS**. `namesChecksum` `95dcb17c...64eb`. New untracked receipt: `sveltekit-frontend/docs/reports/trace-mcp-census-reconcile-01-2026-10-03.json` (not committed). This confirms the 188 figure used in `claude.md`; the two counts are different populations (static scan vs runtime), not a disagreement.
- **What this does NOT establish:** parity between the helper registry (v1, 12 helpers) and MCP tools. The registry's `ownerRef`s are source files, not tool names, and the receipt stores only the names checksum, not the names. No helper-id -> MCP-tool-name map exists.
- **Observation for the vote classification (proposal, not frozen):** the live surface exposes many overlapping retrieval tools, e.g. `atlas_exact_topk_oracle`, `atlas_packet_dense_search`, `atlas_embedding_neighbors`, `search_hybrid`, `search_go_hybrid`, `search_rerank`, `turbovec_rank_chunks`, `kb_hybrid_search`, `trace_search`, `trace_kag_search`, `kag_multi_lane_search`, `search_postgres_fts`, `hypergraph_search`, `graph_pagerank_top`, `atlas_graph_pagerank`, `ops_gpu_topk`, `ops_gpu_pagerank`, `topology_search_*`. These are tool entry points, not fusion votes: an agent that calls several and merges client-side could cast a semantic vote more than once. A governed rule, to be frozen only after a human labels each tool: every retrieval MCP tool is either (a) a lane EXECUTOR under one logical lane, (b) a DIAGNOSTIC/oracle (never a vote, e.g. the exact top-k oracle), or (c) an already-fused entry point (`search_hybrid`, `kb_hybrid_search`), and an agent must not re-fuse (c) with (a). Next step if wanted: persist the live names alongside the checksum and add a labeled `mcp-tool-lane-map` guard in the same style as the helper-vote guard.

### OPENSPEC-DAILY-ANALYSIS-01 review (#12) + subtask 02 dirty-set built (2026-10-03)
- **Verdict recorded:** adopt the daily-pipeline pattern (bounded CPU workers, dirty-set, phase receipts, GPU phases `SKIPPED_HEADROOM` rather than failing, warming only after ranking and only via a canary, dry-run zero-write guarantee); **do not build a second runner.** `scripts/atlas/run-openspec-evidence-fabric-v1.mjs` (`npm run atlas:docs:evidence:pipeline`; 33 stage runs, bounded pool default 2 / cap 3, `cache: DISABLED`, serial dependency order, per-stage receipts) is the runner. `OPENSPEC-DAILY-ANALYSIS-01` = an `--incremental` mode plus the missing stages on that runner, and phase receipts extend its existing per-stage record. No new `openspec:analysis:daily` scheduler, no RabbitMQ consumer, no simdjson yet (the 45 MB ledger builds in ~5 s and parses in seconds; measure first), Valkey descriptors only after the ordinal map and feature revision exist, worker count stays within the existing cap of 3.
- **Finding that changes the plan:** the generated card embeds `REVISION workspace=... source=...` in `contextBlob` and hashes the revisions into `cardId` and `checksum` (`compile-openspec-evidence-cards-v1.mjs` lines 29, 59, 86). Any workspace change would therefore (a) make every card's checksum differ, so checksum-based dirty detection marks all 11,024 cards dirty every time, and (b) put a volatile revision hash string into the text that would be embedded, so the embedding input changes daily for no semantic reason. **Implication for the fixtures above:** the 10/40/100-card fixtures embedded the full `contextBlob` including that revision suffix; their determinism result stands (same input, same output), but they are not representative of the intended recipe, and the 0.80/0.89 weak recall includes revision-string noise. The intended embedding text is `contextBlob` without the revision suffix.
- **Built:** `scripts/atlas/openspec-dirty-set-v1.mjs` (+ `.test.mjs`, 7 tests passing). Revision-independent `contentHash` (claim, predicates, proof state, blockers, contradictions, dependency and receipt refs, embedding text, recipe revision), diff against a baseline snapshot (added / modified / removed / unchanged), `RECIPE_REVISION` bump dirties everything, duplicate keys reported, and `naiveCardChecksumDirty` shown next to the real count to make the revision problem visible (a revision-only bump: naive 3 of 3 dirty, content-hash 0). Dry run by default; `--write-snapshot` writes only `.tmp/atlas/openspec-dirty/baseline.json`. First run on the 11,024-card corpus: `NO_BASELINE_FULL_BUILD` (55,120 representations); after writing the baseline, a rerun is `CLEAN`, 0 dirty, 0 duplicate keys. Real daily churn is not measured yet: the cards report is untracked and has no history to diff, so the first honest number needs two runs on different days.
- **Not done:** the compiler still emits the revision suffix into `contextBlob` (changing the card generator is a separate, deliberate step: either strip it at embedding time via `embeddingText`, as the dirty-set does, or stop emitting it); wiring the dirty-set into the runner as `--incremental`; phase receipts; the task `CandidateOrdinalMap` (still needs the 337 board-only / 1,742 card-only classification).

### OPENSPEC-DAILY-ANALYSIS-01A: dirty-set stage wired into the existing runner (2026-10-03, review #13)
- **Runner extended, not replaced** (`scripts/atlas/run-openspec-evidence-fabric-v1.mjs`): new serial stage **`EVF-05B_DIRTY_SET`** (`openspec-dirty-set-v1.mjs`) between card compilation and feature packets, reading the run's own `evidence-cards-v1.json` via the existing `OPENSPEC_CARDS_PATH` and writing `dirty-set-v1.json` into the run directory via the new `OPENSPEC_DIRTY_SET_OUTPUT`. It is read-only and the runner never passes `--write-snapshot` (a test enforces that); the baseline under `.tmp/atlas/openspec-dirty/` is written only by an explicit command. Worker cap untouched (default 2, hard cap 3, asserted by a test). The runner's entry point is now guarded so its functions can be imported without starting the pipeline.
- **Stage records are additive for every stage:** `durationMs` and `status` (`OK` / `FAILED`) always; an optional `phase` block (`inputRevision`, `outputRevision`, `inputCount`, `outputCount`, `dirtyCount`, `skippedUnchangedCount`, `writesPerformed`) merged from a stage's structured stdout (`phaseFromStdout`, whitelisted fields only). Existing stages print no `phaseReceipt`, so they only gain the two simple fields; phase counters for other stages are not yet emitted.
- **Receipt `atlas.openspec-dirty-set-receipt.v1`:** `sourceRevision`, `cardUniverseChecksum`, `totalCards`, `dirtyCards`, `unchangedCards`, added / removed / modified, `dirtyRepresentations` (dirty x 5), `dirtyKeys` (first 500 in stdout, full list in the receipt file) plus `dirtyKeysChecksum`, and `reasons`: `taskRevisionChanged`, `receiptRevisionChanged`, `embeddingTextChanged`, `embeddingRecipeChanged`, `cardChecksumChanged` (informational: it changes with every workspace revision, so it is never a dirty cause), `unattributed`. **`extractorRevisionChanged`, `ontologyRevisionChanged`, `featureRevisionChanged` are `null`, not 0**: no NLP extractor, ontology or feature-matrix revision exists yet to compare, so the review's seven-reason list is honestly five-of-seven tracked. Statuses: `NO_BASELINE_FULL_BUILD`, `CLEAN`, `DIRTY`, `RECIPE_CHANGED_FULL_REBUILD`, `BASELINE_FORMAT_CHANGED_REBASELINE` (new `HASH_FORMAT` guard: an older-format baseline is re-baselined, not compared hash-to-hash).
- **Verified (not by running the full pipeline):** 16 node tests (dirty-set 11 + runner 5, plus the 4 existing stage-pool tests all green: 20 total earlier run), `node --check` on the runner, and the stage invoked env-driven exactly as the runner would: first run full build / format re-baseline, rerun `CLEAN` (0 dirty, 11,024 skipped, 55,120 representations avoided). **The full pipeline was deliberately NOT run**: its stages overwrite tracked `docs/reports/openspec-evidence-*.json` files that another session has modified (162 changed or untracked report paths in the tree), and a read-only claim about the runner cannot be proven without that side effect being reviewed. Run it only after those files are committed or isolated.
- **Next (unchanged order):** classify the 337 board-only + 1,742 card-only rows -> task `CandidateOrdinalMap` -> deterministic `CandidateFeatureMatrix`; only then embeddings (dirty cards x representations only), graph / hypergraph, GPU (`SKIPPED_HEADROOM`), Valkey, tournament. No simdjson, RabbitMQ consumer, Valkey writes, GPU work or tournament execution was added.

### Embedding-input canonicalization frozen at the embedding boundary (2026-10-03, review #14); generator unchanged
- **Decision applied:** the EvidenceCard generator is NOT changed (the card stays the full audit artifact and may carry `REVISION workspace=... source=...`). The revision decoration is stripped only at the embedding-input boundary, in one shared module `scripts/atlas/lib/openspec-embedding-input-v1.mjs` (`normalizeCardText`, `buildEmbeddingInputV1`, `documentString`, `EMBEDDING_INPUT_RECIPE_REVISION`). `OpenSpecEmbeddingInputV1` = `{sourceCardId, sourceCardRevision, sourceCardChecksum, representationRecipeRevision, title, normalizedText, normalizedTextChecksum, embeddingCacheKey}`. The cache key hashes recipe + title + normalized-text checksum (+ optional `modelArtifactRevision`) and deliberately excludes cardId, card checksum and revisions. The dirty-set and the fixture both call this module, so they cannot drift.
- **Contract tests (8, passing):** workspace-revision-only change -> same normalized text, same checksum, same cache key, dirty-set `CLEAN` (while the card checksum and `sourceCardRevision` do move); semantic change at the same revision -> different text / checksum / cache key and `embeddingTextChanged`; cache key moves with model artifact and recipe revision.
- **Real-corpus findings that shaped the regex (11,024 cards):** 10,657 blobs end with the full revision suffix; **367 have no suffix because the card compiler's token budget truncated the blob first**; **75 have the suffix itself truncated** (workspace hash cut short, `source=` missing or partial), which the first regex missed; a few are cut inside the word (`REVISI`, `REVISIO`, `REVISION`). The tolerant strip plus a word-cut rule now leave **0 cards containing a workspace hash**; 79 cards still contain the plain word `REVISION` as genuine card content (left alone), and 2 blobs end in a bare `RE` that cannot be told from a real word (deliberately not stripped). Average blob length 699 -> 534 chars. Because the strip rule tightened after the first baseline, the dirty-set flagged exactly those 75 (then 77 after the word-cut rule) as `embeddingTextChanged`: correct behavior, and harmless because nothing has been embedded under the old rule. The baseline under `.tmp/atlas/openspec-dirty/` was re-written; rerun is `CLEAN` (0 dirty, 11,024 skipped).
- **Fixture numbers, restated:** the earlier 10/40/100-card runs keep their determinism result but are recipe-contaminated (each input carried the shared revision hash string). Re-run on the canonical text, same 40 cards in the same order: `FIXTURE_OK`, min repeat cosine 0.9999999999999998, same-change recall@3 **97/120 = 0.808** (contaminated: 0.800), mean top-3 cosine **0.850** (contaminated: 0.866), and **109 of 120 top-3 neighbours identical** to the contaminated run. So the shared revision string inflated similarity a little and moved about 9% of neighbours; it did not change the weak-label recall materially. Still a diagnostic, not a quality baseline or a truth set; the canonical 40-card manifest is `.tmp/atlas/openspec-embed-fixture-40-canonical/` (vectors sha256 `696621cb...ffc24`) and replaces the old ones as the reference.
- **Order from here (unchanged):** classify 337 board-only + 1,742 card-only -> freeze the task/evidence universe -> task `CandidateOrdinalMap` -> deterministic `CandidateFeatureMatrix` -> only then the 55,120-representation initial build (dirty cards x representations afterwards).

### EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01 reframed as a bounded live-path gate; census + ratchet guard built (2026-10-03, review #15)
- **Reconciliation:** my earlier "deferred by evidence" note (review #4) is superseded for the gate as the operator now defines it: closure of LIVE bypasses only, not a repo-wide rewrite; `unprompted_legacy` stays wherever a corpus recipe is not independently qualified; classes `LIVE_CONSUMER / FIXTURE / TEST / DIAGNOSTIC / LEGACY / DORMANT / DEAD`, only `LIVE_CONSUMER` blocks. Reported by the other session and NOT re-verified here: `executeEmbeddingInputV1` wired into dual-lane, reranked-search and tags/search (with statutes, precedents, glossary: six routes preserving `unprompted_legacy`); embedding adapter/retrieval 15 PASS; HyperRAG bounded reader/fusion 41 PASS (max 4096 incidence rows, fail closed); OpenSpec strict validation PASS for its change; repo-wide diff check INCOMPLETE (pre-existing dirty simd-bridge log). HyperRAG: `MULTIHOP_LINEAGE_UNPROVEN` stands (reported 62,802 hyperedges on an old workspace/graph revision plus revisionless relationship/snapshot tables); the exact-revision multihop proof, the stale/revisionless classification and any Graphify refresh are NOT started here.
- **Built (read-only):** `scripts/atlas/audit-embedding-direct-endpoints-v1.mjs` (+ test, 5 passing). Scans 5,075 production source files under `sveltekit-frontend/src` (tests/specs excluded), classifies every file that references a direct embedding endpoint (`/v1/embeddings`, `/api/embeddings`, `/api/embed` other than a bare relative call to the app's own route), and uses the real import graph (with `$lib` and relative resolution) to test reachability from 1,333 route/hook/service-worker/MCP roots and 13 worker roots. 2 s runtime.
- **Census result (125 files):** `LIVE_CONSUMER` **92** (reachable from a route/hook root), `UNREACHABLE_DORMANT_CANDIDATE` **18**, `INTERNAL_ROUTE_ONLY` 6 (call the app's own `/api/embed`, i.e. the facade, not a bypass), `TRANSPORT_OWNER` 5 (declared adapter/transport files), `DIAGNOSTIC` 4 (health, semantic-health, system-configuration). Per-file rows: `.tmp/atlas/embedding-caller-census-v1.json`.
- **What the 92 means (caveats, important):** (1) the census counts ANY direct embedding-endpoint reference, not only `semantic_768` corpus/query paths; many are 768-d `embeddinggemma` calls for caches, tagging, memory, etc. with their own recipes, so "92 live bypasses to migrate" is the upper bound of the universe to classify, not a migration backlog; (2) reachability is module-level import reachability from a very broad root set (1,333 files), an over-approximation of "executed in production"; barrel imports inflate it; (3) the 18 dormant candidates are candidates only (dynamic import strings and process-launched scripts are not all captured); (4) it does not read recipes: classifying each live file's recipe (`unprompted_legacy` vs prefixed) and which corpus it targets is the remaining manual step, and the `semantic_768` subset is the part the operator's bounded gate actually concerns. **Finding:** the "transport owner" layer is itself plural: the live set includes `lib/server/ai/ollama-client.ts`, `lib/utils/ollama.ts`, `lib/server/retrieval/embedding-service.ts`, `embedding-models.ts`, `lib/server/rg-atlas/embed.ts`, `lib/server/grpc/embedding-client.ts`, `lib/server/batch-embedder.ts`, `lib/server/atlas/docs/semantic-768-client.ts` and others that each carry their own endpoint/fallback policy, which is exactly "a live caller silently owns transport/fallback policy" (gate item 4). They need an explicit owner-vs-consumer decision (the registry `runtime-ownership-registry.json` has no embedding capability entry yet).
- **Guard (ratchet, not a migration):** `--check` fails when a file that references a direct embedding endpoint is neither a declared transport owner nor in the tolerated baseline `docs/architecture/embedding-direct-endpoint-baseline.json` (written once with the explicit `--write-baseline`; 110 files = the live + dormant-candidate set). A NEW bypass fails; migrating a file and removing it from the baseline is how the list shrinks (`resolvedSinceBaseline` reports it). Verified: `--check` exits 0 on the current tree. Not wired into CI or an npm script. Writing the baseline froze 110 files as tolerated debt; that is a deliberate acceptance of today's state, not a statement that they are correct.
- **Next in the operator's order:** step 2 migrate only live `semantic_768` bypasses (needs the per-file recipe/corpus read for the 92; start with files under `lib/server/retrieval/`, `routes/api/retrieval/` and `lib/server/ace/`), then rerun the graph revision audit and classify the stale / revisionless HyperRAG populations before any Graphify refresh.

### EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01: shared provider executor + first migration (2026-10-03; other embedding session paused by the operator)
- **Finding about the existing "migrated" routes:** `executeEmbeddingInputV1` (the other session's adapter, currently untracked) binds the recipe (`mode`, prompt revision, checksums) and validates the returned vector, but its `executor` callback is supplied by each caller, so dual-lane / reranked-search / tags / statutes / precedents / glossary still hand-roll the Ollama URL, model id, timeout and failure shape inline. That satisfies "preserve the recipe explicitly" and "vector validation", but NOT gate item 4 (no live caller silently owns transport/fallback policy). That is why the census still lists them as direct-endpoint files.
- **Built:** `src/lib/server/embedding/embedding-provider-executor-v1.ts` (+ spec, 8 tests passing): `createProviderEmbeddingExecutorV1` / `executeProviderEmbeddingV1`. One HTTP executor that centralizes endpoint shape per provider (`ollama` -> `/api/embeddings`; `llama_cpp_gguf` -> `/v1/embeddings`; `onnx_directml` and a missing base URL fail closed), model id, a 30 s timeout and the `EMBEDDING_HTTP_<status>` failure; provider from `resolveEmbeddingProviderV1()` by default, or passed explicitly, with an optional base-URL override for config-driven callers. **Deliberately no cache, retry or backend fallback**: that is the `embed.ts` facade (cache + in-flight dedup + circuit breaker + gRPC/QUIC/HTTP chain), whose Go-service backend has no proven parity with the receipt-bound GGUF, so moving callers onto it would silently change their backend. The recipe remains the caller's explicit `mode`. Declared a transport owner in the census guard.
- **First migration:** `lib/server/retrieval/unified-orchestrator.ts::generateEmbedding` (only exposed through the file's default-export object; no external callers; no test mocks it) now calls `executeProviderEmbeddingV1` with `mode: 'unprompted_legacy'` and the provider/host/port it used before (Ollama at `config.ollama.host:port`, `embeddinggemma:latest`). Behavior deltas, stated: it now has a 30 s timeout (none before) and stricter vector validation (finite values, non-zero norm; previously only length 768). `unified-orchestrator.spec.ts` 4/4 passes; not run: anything that exercises a live Ollama.
- **Guard state:** census now LIVE_CONSUMER 91 (was 92), TRANSPORT_OWNER 6, dormant candidates 18; baseline shrunk 110 -> 109 (`docs/architecture/embedding-direct-endpoint-baseline.json` regenerated with `--write-baseline`); `--check` passes.
- **Deliberately NOT migrated yet:** `lib/server/retrieval/auto-backfill.ts`, `embedding-models.ts`, `lib/server/ace/*` and the other live files. Several are corpus WRITERS (stricter vector validation would turn an accepted zero or non-finite vector into a thrown error), some carry their own model env (`OLLAMA_EMBED_MODEL`), and each needs its recipe/corpus read first. `go-retrieval-facade.ts` calls the app's own `/api/embed` (facade, not a bypass).
- **Commit hazard:** the new executor imports `embedding-execution-adapter-v1.ts` and `embedding-provider-v1.ts` contract types; the adapter and its specs are UNTRACKED work from the paused session, and `unified-orchestrator.ts`, `embedding-contract-768.ts`, `embedding-service.ts` etc. carry that session's uncommitted edits. Committing only my files would leave HEAD with an executor that imports a file HEAD does not have. Commit the adapter (with its owner's approval) first, or together, not separately.

### Lane A: embedding caller census v2 (tightened classes, 2026-10-03)
- **Classes** (`audit-embedding-direct-endpoints-v1.mjs`, 8 tests): `LIVE_DIRECT_CALLER`, `LIVE_WRAPPER` (an exported embed/vector/encode function holding the transport call), `TRANSPORT_OWNER` (declared list), `ROUTE_REACHABLE_NONCALLER` (endpoint string present but not used in a request: constants, route registries, SSE, comments), `INTERNAL_ROUTE_ONLY` (calls the app's own `/api/embed` facade), `FIXTURE`, `DIAGNOSTIC`, `DORMANT` (unreachable from any route, hook or worker root). `TEST` files are excluded from the scan; `LEGACY` / `DEAD` stay manual labels because the import graph cannot see process-launched scripts or dynamic import strings.
- **Method corrections found by spot-checking, not assumed:** the first v2 pass classified 44 files as non-callers; sampling showed repo files call through an `ollamaFetch(...)` wrapper (and some store the URL in a constant used elsewhere in the file), so the detector now matches any `*Fetch(` helper and follows one variable hop. Result moved from 44 non-callers to 10.
- **Result (5,075 files scanned, 125 with a reference):** `LIVE_DIRECT_CALLER` **78** (33 route handlers + 45 library files), `LIVE_WRAPPER` **3** (`lib/ai/ollama-config.ts`, `lib/server/ai/ollama-client.ts`, `lib/server/cache/semantic-cache.ts`, each exporting its own `generateEmbedding`), `DORMANT` 18, `ROUTE_REACHABLE_NONCALLER` 10, `INTERNAL_ROUTE_ONLY` 6, `TRANSPORT_OWNER` 6, `DIAGNOSTIC` 4. So the "92 live" earlier was an overcount in both directions; the live caller set that matters to the gate is 78 + 3. Still the upper bound for `semantic_768`: it counts any embedding endpoint call, not only corpus/query paths of that representation.
- **Remaining non-callers worth a human look:** `routes/api/v1/agentic/tool-loop/+server.ts` (endpoint inside a template that the heuristic did not follow to a request) and `lib/utils/ollama.ts` (a URL builder, effectively a transport helper). The other 8 are registries, SSE, config and comments.
- **Ratchet:** guards `LIVE_DIRECT_CALLER`, `LIVE_WRAPPER` and `DORMANT` (a dormant file with a call can become live); owners, non-callers, fixtures, diagnostics and facade-only routes pass. Baseline `docs/architecture/embedding-direct-endpoint-baseline.json` is now schema v2 with 99 tolerated files (it was 109 under the looser definition; the drop is reclassification of non-callers, not migration). `--check` passes.
- **Finding for the transport-owner decision (gate item 4):** besides the declared owners, `ollama-client.ts`, `semantic-cache.ts`, `ollama-config.ts` and `lib/utils/ollama.ts` each carry their own embedding transport and are imported elsewhere; `ollamaFetch` is a fourth wrapper layer under many callers. Which of them becomes THE executor owner (the new `embedding-provider-executor-v1.ts` is the candidate) is an operator decision; until then every caller of those wrappers still has transport policy outside the executor.
- **Parallel-lane note:** another session is concurrently editing `run-openspec-evidence-fabric-v1.mjs` and its test (an `--incremental --dry-run` mode with `parseRunnerArgs`); I left both untouched. Lanes B (task-card ownership), C (HyperRAG lineage) and E (337 / 1,742 classification) are not started by this session.

### Lane B: task-card owner reconciliation, ranker side implemented (2026-10-03)
- **The triage change already owns this.** `openspec/changes/parent-atlas-openspec-task-triage-pipeline` (proposal, design, spec delta, tasks; valid strict change) defines `TaskCardV1` (`atlas.openspec-task-card.v1`, corpus `docs/reports/openspec-task-cards-v1.json`, 10,455 cards over 94 ledgers, 8.3 MB, `canonicalAuthority:false`), `ReportArtifactManifestV1`, the triage selector `selectOpenSpecTaskCardsV1` and task `2.6 WB-TASKCARD-OWNER-01`, whose audit is `docs/reports/wb-taskcard-owner-v1-20261003.md` (`INCOMPLETE`). **My earlier plan for an `OpenSpecTaskCardV1` projection (WB-CARD-01) is withdrawn: `TaskCardV1` is the surviving projection; I build no second card family.** That also settles the open question about my EvidenceCard decision: the 11,024 generated EvidenceCards stay the evidence unit; `TaskCardV1` is the task-oriented projection and it already joins evidence by exact span, task hash and workspace revision.
- **Agreed with the audit's findings:** (1) my `rank-open-tasks-v1.mjs` applied its own eligibility to the raw Workboard inventory instead of consuming the selector (true; fixed below); (2) `selectOpenSpecTaskCardsV1` admits `REVIEW_REQUIRED` by default, so it is a retrieval selector, not an admitted-only one (8,705 of 10,455 cards are `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, mostly `CHECKED_WITHOUT_PROOF`); (3) the two manifests are different layers (report-artifact entries vs one ledger's shards). My shard manifest keeps schema `atlas.workboard-report-manifest.v1`; use "WorkboardReportManifestV1" only as a descriptive name.
- **Parity measured, not assumed** (fresh Workboard 22:32Z vs the corpus built 21:04Z): all 772 ranker candidates join exactly to a card by (file, line), every one with card `state: OPEN`; 762 are retrievalState `CURRENT` and 10 are `REVIEW_REQUIRED` (7 `AMBIGUOUS_TASK_IDENTITY`, 3 `EVIDENCE_RECEIPT_STALE`). Of the 1,398 `CURRENT` cards, 762 are ranker candidates; the other 636 are OPEN cards the ranker rightly excludes on execution gating (gate not ready or write-gated), which cards do not carry. Card `state` counts (OPEN 2,728 / WAITING 837 / CHECKED 6,890) match the Workboard (ACTIONABLE 2,730 / WAITING 840 / DONE 6,890) to within timing.
- **Boundary implemented (ranker side, 17 tests passing):** `lib/task-card-cohort-v1.mjs` defines the frozen cohort `atlas.task-card-cohort.v1` (locators `{stableKey, sourcePath, sourceLine, taskRevision}`, the admitting `retrievalStates` / `states`, per-file `taskFileHashes`, checksum, `canonicalAuthority:false`), a reference exporter `buildTaskCardCohortV1(taskCardCorpus, {retrievalStates:['CURRENT'], states:['OPEN']})` and `bindCohortToLedgerV1`, which fails closed: a tampered cohort throws, and any task file whose hash differs between the cohort and the ledger (or is absent from it) is excluded wholesale. `rank-open-tasks-v1.mjs` gained `--cohort <file>` / `--cohort-from-corpus <task-cards.json>`: **lifecycle admission comes from the cohort; execution gating (gate / controller / mutation class) stays in the ranker** because cards do not carry it; output records `eligibilitySource` (`FROZEN_TASK_CARD_COHORT` or `RAW_WORKBOARD_INVENTORY`) and the cohort checksum. Without a cohort the old behavior is unchanged, labelled as raw inventory. Parity test: cohort mode only narrows the legacy set.
- **Result on real data:** cohort mode ranks **762** (legacy 772); the **top 10 is identical**; the 10 dropped are the `REVIEW_REQUIRED` cards (legacy ranks 11, 24, 165, 196, 204-206, 211, 212, 307); no new candidates; no stale or missing files. ### HyperRAG reader production-reachability trace (2026-10-04) — caller now revision-gated
- **Production path confirmed and hardened.** `retrieval/search-runtime.ts` calls `lookupHypergraphNeighbors()` after ranking; it now requires every final packet to carry the same non-empty `workspace_revision` and `graph_revision`, then calls `readKagHypergraphNeighborsStrictV1(canonicalIds, snapshot)`. Missing or mixed revisions skip the lookup; strict read errors remain non-blocking to search and omit `provenance.hypergraphNeighbors`.
- **But it can match nothing today** (live SELECTs, 2026-10-04): `atlas_hyperedges` 62,802 rows, all with `contract_hyperedge_id`; `atlas_hyperedge_members` 125,604 rows; members whose `member_id` equals an `atlas_packets.packet_key`: **0**; equals an `atlas_packets.source_ref`: **0**; `atlas_ontology_linked_tuples`: **0** rows. So the production lookup returns an empty neighbour set for every query: harmless now, but it is an unrevisioned read path. If any writer later populates matching `member_id`s (or tuples), stale rows would flow into `provenance` with no revision check.
- **Classification:** old unchecked production lookup = `EMPTY_VALID` today, `UNSAFE_IF_POPULATED`; new production caller = `REVISION_GATED`, currently `NOT_EXECUTED` because the SearchRuntime `FeatureEnvelope` hydration path does not yet populate `graph_revision`. It also has no qualified current packet-incidence rows to return, per the live census above.
- **Proof:** SearchRuntime regression proves a shared exact snapshot reaches the strict reader and attaches its result; a missing snapshot does not call the reader or attach neighbors. `search-runtime.spec.ts` (17), taxonomy shadow (1), strict reader (10), and quick-hop (5) pass. This is a caller-safety proof, not `MULTIHOP_LINEAGE_PROVEN`.
- **Remaining for `HYPERRAG-INCIDENCE-OWNER-01` / `MULTIHOP-FILL-01`:** identify the admitted writer for packet-keyed, revision-qualified incidence; carry authoritative `graph_revision` through candidate hydration; prove a bounded live cohort at the same workspace and graph revisions. No Graphify refresh or database writes were performed.

### NAME-ENV-01 prerequisite DONE (2026-10-04, commit `7d0b59e319`)
- `normalizeLlamaServerBaseUrlV1` now lives in `ai/llama-server-model-resolver.ts` (no `ENV` import, no import-time throw) and is re-exported from `llm/runtime-contract.ts`; body unchanged; `runtime-contract.spec.ts` 4/4. Readers can now import the pure function without the contract's `ROTORQUANT_MODEL_PATH` requirement.
- **First reader inspected, deliberately not changed:** `atlas/feature-doc-enrichment.ts:395-397` writes `modelPlan.summarizerModel = GEMMA4_MODEL || 'gemma4'` and `summarizerEndpoint = LLAMA_SERVER_URL || GEMMA4_URL || ...` into an enrichment **plan object** (recorded output, possibly checksummed downstream), so swapping the chain would change plan contents, not just the env lookup. It has no spec. Readers split into three kinds and each kind needs a different treatment: (a) plain request-time URL/model lookups (safe to point at the normalizer/`LLM_MODEL_ID`), (b) readers that record the value into plans/packets/receipts (changes recorded output; needs the owning schema's view), (c) readers where `GEMMA4_URL` may legitimately target a non-llama-server host. The 31+33 files have not been sorted into (a)/(b)/(c); that classification is the next step before any bulk migration.

### NAME-ENV-01A proofs + reader classification (2026-10-04)
- **Added:** `resolveOrnithRequestModelV1(target)` in `ai/model-ids.ts` (`LLAMA_SERVER` -> canonical `ornith-1.5-9b`, request `ornith-1.5-9b`; `BIFROST_OPENAI` -> canonical `ornith-1.5-9b`, request `openai/ornith-1.5-9b`; anything else throws `UNKNOWN_ORNITH_TRANSPORT_TARGET`) and `ai/ornith-llama-boundary.spec.ts`. With the runtime-contract spec and the Gemma-literal guard: 3 files, 17 tests pass. Proofs: normalizer cases (bare, `/`, `/v1`, `/v1/`, case/whitespace, stability); resolver imports with `ROTORQUANT_*` unset and `ENV` mocked empty; `runtime-contract.ts` still throws `ROTORQUANT_MODEL_PATH is required` in that setup; direct vs Bifrost ids; fail-closed on `OLLAMA`.
- **Reader classification (read-only, `git grep GEMMA4_URL` outside tests/docs, ~35 code files): zero qualify for migration under the NAME-ENV-01A policy.**
  - `src` (2): `ace/hermes-mastra-orchestrator.ts` only has a static field *named* `GEMMA4_URL` initialised from `LLAMA_SERVER_URL || default` (already neutral; it appends `/chat/completions` to a `/v1` base, so normalising would change its URL shape); `atlas/feature-doc-enrichment.ts:395-397` records model/endpoint into an enrichment plan (recorded output, class b).
  - `scripts/**` and `sveltekit-frontend/scripts/**` (~30 standalone `.mjs/.mts`): read **only** `GEMMA4_URL` (no neutral name, so "preserve precedence" leaves nothing to centralise), cannot import the TypeScript normalizer without a TS runner, and several set a **full endpoint** (`.../v1/chat/completions` in `live-adapter-proof.mjs`, `classify-qdrant-missing-packets`, `generate-gemma4-weak-labels`, `phase4-gemma4-batch-labels`) where base-URL normalisation does not apply. Those named `gemma4-*`/`phase*-summaries` write model output and summaries into stored artifacts (class b); `phase7-triton-batch-summaries.mjs` targets Triton and `test-gpu-retrieval-summary-fanout.mjs` already prefers `GEMMA4_URL` over `LLAMA_SERVER_URL` (reverse precedence), so neither is an ordinary lane caller.
  - **Conclusion:** the URL-normalisation duplication this policy targets does not exist in these readers; the prerequisite (pure normalizer, import-safe) is done and tested, and the remaining legacy-name cleanup is a *B* problem (env-name resolution) that the policy tells us not to bundle. No reader was changed. `NAME-ENV-01` stays open for B (per-script decisions, owner sign-off for any script whose `GEMMA4_URL` may target a non-llama-server host).

### NAME-ENV-01B decision sheet: `GEMMA4_URL` script readers (2026-10-04, read-only; grep of defaults and API shapes, no file changed)
Local `.env` has `GEMMA4_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8090` (same as `LLAMA_SERVER_URL`), so these all reach llama-server here; other deployments unknown. 33 code files:
- **llama-server base URL, `.../chat/completions` appended, default `:8090` (21):** `phase7-rabbitmq-batch-worker.mjs`, `phase7-simple-summarizer.mjs`, `scripts/atlas/{graphify-summary-phase1, phase1-rabbitmq-batch-summarizer, phase7-speed-test, retrieval-e2e-benchmark, stage1-2-worker-pool, stage1-simple-backfill, validate-phase1-readiness, graphify-summary-ranking-pipeline}.mjs`, `scripts/phase85/{p8-semantic-diff-batch-langextract, p9-langextract-agentic-error-fixing, p9-langextract-gpu-accelerated}.mjs`, `scripts/startup/p9-langextract-agentic-startup.mjs`, `sveltekit-frontend/scripts/atlas/{batch-summarize-chunks, phase7-rabbitmq-summary-queue}.mjs`, `phase6-synthesis.mts` (default has `/v1`), `ecosystem.config.cjs` (env block). Candidate for a neutral-first read (`LLAMA_SERVER_URL ?? GEMMA4_URL`) once an owner agrees; do not add the neutral name as a first-choice silently (it changes precedence for hosts that set both differently).
- **No default (read from env or argv only) (7):** `scripts/atlas/{prove-retrieval-summarization-flow, summarize-rank-embed-centroids, summary-ranking-retrieval-pipeline, test-gpu-retrieval-summary-fanout}.mjs`, `scripts/opencode/{gemma4_summarizer, gemma4-adapter, summarize_cards_gemma4}.mjs`. Three already pair it with `LLAMA_SERVER_URL`; `test-gpu-retrieval-summary-fanout.mjs` prefers `GEMMA4_URL` first (reverse precedence).
- **Full endpoint, not a base URL (4):** `live-adapter-proof.mjs`, `classify-qdrant-missing-packets.mjs.mjs` (note the doubled `.mjs.mjs` filename), `generate-gemma4-weak-labels.mts`, `phase4-gemma4-batch-labels.mts` default to `http://127.0.0.1:8090/v1/chat/completions`; base-URL normalisation does not apply.
- **Triton (2):** both `phase7-triton-batch-summaries.mjs` copies prefer `LLAMA_SERVER_URL ?? GEMMA4_URL` but target Triton in their flow; owned by the Triton lane.
- **Not a runtime caller (1):** `export-packets-for-colab.mts` embeds a Python snippet (`GEMMA4_URL = "http://localhost:11434/api/chat"  # Ollama local or Colab GPU`, line ~158) in a template it writes out for a Colab notebook; it is generated script text, not a live Ollama bypass, but the template still teaches Ollama `/api/chat` as the default.
- **Not scanned for vision use:** none of these files was read for image inputs; a `VISION?` marker found none, but the grep was filename-agnostic and shallow.
- **Decision requested:** (1) approve a neutral-first `LLAMA_SERVER_URL ?? GEMMA4_URL` read for the 21 base-URL scripts (one directory per commit, each run with `--dry-run`/syntax check where available); (2) confirm the 4 full-endpoint scripts stay as they are; (3) decide whether the Colab template should default to llama-server.

### NAME-ENV-01B batch 1 (2026-10-04, operator chose option 1: neutral-first read for the base-URL scripts)
- **Migrated (9):** `phase7-simple-summarizer.mjs`, `scripts/atlas/{graphify-summary-phase1, graphify-summary-ranking-pipeline, phase1-rabbitmq-batch-summarizer, phase7-speed-test, stage1-2-worker-pool, validate-phase1-readiness}.mjs`, `sveltekit-frontend/scripts/atlas/{batch-summarize-chunks, phase7-rabbitmq-summary-queue}.mjs`. Each had the identical line `process.env.GEMMA4_URL || 'http://127.0.0.1:8090'`; now `(LLAMA_SERVER_URL || GEMMA4_URL || default).trim()` with trailing `/` and `/v1` stripped inline (the scripts append `/v1/chat/completions` or `/v1/models`, and `LLAMA_SERVER_URL` is `.../v1` in one local `.env`, so a bare swap would have produced `/v1/v1/...`). `.mjs` cannot import the TypeScript normalizer, so the two `replace` calls are inlined; they are the same ones as `normalizeLlamaServerBaseUrlV1`.
- **Equivalence audit (old -> new effective URL):** neither set: `:8090` -> `:8090`; `GEMMA4_URL` only: unchanged; `LLAMA_SERVER_URL` only: default -> that URL (old ignored it); **both set: `GEMMA4_URL` -> `LLAMA_SERVER_URL` (intentional precedence change, the approved neutral-first order)**; `/v1`, `/v1/`, `/` suffix: stripped. On this machine both names resolve to `http://127.0.0.1:8090`, so the local effective endpoint is unchanged. `behaviorEquivalent` is true locally and for hosts that set only one name or equal values; false by design for hosts that set the two names to different servers.
- **Verification:** `node --check` OK on all nine; the precedence table above was computed with an inline copy of the expression. NOT run: none of the scripts was executed (they call llama-server or RabbitMQ).
- **Left unchanged on purpose:** `phase7-rabbitmq-batch-worker.mjs` (hardcoded constant, no env read; adding one is a new behavior), `retrieval-e2e-benchmark.mjs` (reads `LLAMA_URL`), `stage1-simple-backfill.mjs` (already `LLAMA_SERVER_URL` only), `phase6-synthesis.mts` and `ecosystem.config.cjs` (a `/v1`-based default and an env block), the 7 no-default scripts, the 4 full-endpoint scripts, the Triton pair, and the Colab template.

### Request-model identity fix + mmproj correction (2026-10-04, operator: "/models/ornith1.5 .gguf, not this llama-server 8090 loaded")
- **Probe (read-only):** `GET :8090/v1/models` -> one model `ornith-1.5-9b`; `GET :8090/props` -> `model_path = models\ornith-1_5-9b-ad-q5_k-q4_k\hforf.gguf`, alias `ornith-1.5-9b` (matches `.env` `ROTORQUANT_MODEL_PATH`, `LLAMA_SERVER_MODEL`). The loaded identity is the **alias**; a GGUF filename is neither the alias nor what `/v1/models` lists.

Rule used: replace only a `model:` line whose body also holds `messages` next to a `fetch`/`chat/completions`. `node --check` OK on all; nothing executed.
- **Deliberately not changed (recorded provenance, not request models):** `export-gemma4-summaries-ndjson.mjs:138` (`provenance.model`), `build-summary-envelopes-from-tuples.mjs:478`, the `model_name:` fields in `backfill-summary-layers-from-chunks.mjs` / `import-gemma4-summaries.mjs`, `gemma4-parent-atlas-summaries.mjs`, `run-packet-enrichment-lanes.mjs`, `offline-summary-pipeline.mjs` (default feeds a recorded value), `apply-artifact-tier-classifications.mjs` (artifact registry label), `audit-phase17-21-workstation.mjs`, the Python `phase8-step3-langextract-entities.py` and `start-langextract-gemma4.mjs`. Rewriting stored provenance for naming consistency is out of policy.
- **Correction of an earlier claim:** this ledger and the root `CLAUDE.md` said no `mmproj-Ornith-1.5-9B-*.gguf` exists. **`models/mmproj-Ornith-1.5-9B-BF16.gguf` exists** (also a generic `models/mmproj-F16.gguf`). It is not loaded (`/props` modalities: vision false). The vision blocker is therefore a **launch configuration** decision (start llama-server with `--mmproj` / `TURBO_MMPROJ_PATH` and the memory budget for it on the 8 GiB card), not a missing artifact; whether the projector matches the `hforf.gguf` weights is unverified. Loading it needs a restart of the `:8090` server, which was NOT done.

### Vision-projector feasibility (2026-10-04, read-only; nothing loaded or restarted)
- **Pairing is already recorded, not unverified:** `models/model-manifest.json` has `ORNITH_VISION_PRODUCTION` (`hforf-gguf` + `ornith-1.5-mmproj`, launcher profile `ornith-1.5-vlm`, `compatibility: EXACT_FAMILY_ONLY`, `status: PRODUCTION_PROFILE`) and artifact `ornith-1.5-mmproj` (BF16, 921,704,672 bytes, sha256 `626f9f90…58e1d`, verified 2026-09-05 against the Hugging Face ETag); a Gemma4 projector on these weights is `NON_PRODUCTION`, `loadAllowed: False`. `scripts/launch-turboquant.ps1` already implements the `ornith-1.5-vlm` profile (`ORNITH_MMPROJ_PATH`). This corrects my wording a few entries up ("whether the projector matches ... is unverified"): the manifest asserts it; it has not been run on this host.
- **The real blocker is VRAM:** `nvidia-smi` shows 7,912 of 8,192 MiB in use, about 280 MiB free, against a 922 MB projector plus vision compute buffers. Loading it alongside the current server needs headroom first (lower `TURBO_CTX`/KV, stop another GPU consumer, or accept a smaller profile); the `ornith-1.5-vlm` profile's memory budget has not been measured here.
- **Not done / needs the operator:** restarting `:8090` with `TURBO_PROFILE=ornith-1.5-vlm` (interrupts anything using it, and the GPU is already full). A bare "yes continue" is not taken as authorization for that restart. After a successful vision launch, `/props` must show `vision:true` before the caption script, `ocr/hybrid.ts` `vlm-ocr` and `vlm-evidence-analyzer.ts` are repointed.

### Vision profile launched on :8090 (2026-10-04, operator-authorized restart; supersedes the "VLM lane has no vision model" and "VRAM blocker" entries above)
- **Done:** gracefully stopped PID 51788 (`--port 8090`; the Ollama runner PID 9608 on `:55990` was not touched), unloaded Ollama's idle `embeddinggemma` runner (`ollama stop`, ~750 MiB freed; reloads on demand), and relaunched with `scripts/launch-turboquant.ps1 -Detached -StartupProfile ornith-1.5-vlm` and `LLAMA_ARG_MMPROJ_OFFLOAD=0` (llama-server env for `--no-mmproj-offload`: the projector stays on the CPU). No launcher edit.
- **Verified:** `/health` 200 after ~21 s; `/props` alias `ornith-1.5-9b`, `modalities {vision: true, audio: false}`, `n_ctx 65536`, `supports_system_role true`; `/v1/models` lists only `ornith-1.5-9b`. A real request with a 64x64 solid-red PNG returned `"Red"` (finish `stop`), and a text request returned `TEXT_OK`. `:8085/health` went from `degraded` to `healthy`, `vlm_ocr:true`, `vlm_vision:"true"` (same server behind it).
- **VRAM is tight:** after the launch `nvidia-smi` shows 7,791 of 8,192 MiB used, ~234 MiB free. The old text-only server needed ~6.4 GiB; everything else on the GPU (desktop apps, overlay, WebView) held ~0.9-1.65 GiB. **Side effect:** Ollama's embedding model will try to reload onto the GPU on the next embedding request (needs ~700 MiB; only ~234 MiB free), so the first embeddings may fall back to CPU or be slow/fail until memory is freed (close GPU-heavy desktop apps, or lower `TURBO_CTX`). Not yet tested.
- **Not done:** the consumers are not repointed: `caption-screenshots-gemma4.mjs` still posts to Ollama `/api/generate` (broken independent of this), `ocr/hybrid.ts::extractTextVlm` and `analysis/vlm-evidence-analyzer.ts` already target the llama-server `/v1/chat/completions` path and should now be testable; no image-quality or OCR-accuracy evaluation was run (one colour-patch smoke only). Rollback if needed: stop the `:8090` PID and relaunch `-StartupProfile ornith-1.5`.
- **Follow-up test of the side effect above (2026-10-04):** one `POST :11434/api/embed` (`embeddinggemma:latest`, `keep_alive 5m`) returned a 768-dim vector in ~6 s (cold reload), `ollama ps` shows `100% GPU`, `:8090` stayed `health 200`, and `nvidia-smi` shows 7,843 of 8,192 MiB used (~182 MiB free). So both models coexist right now, but with almost no headroom (Windows may be paging the difference into shared memory; not measured). Treat any new GPU job as likely to fail until memory is freed.

### dev:gpu vision opt-in (2026-10-04)
- **Finding first:** `npm run dev:gpu` (`scripts/startup/dev-gpu-runtime.mjs`) always launched `launch-turboquant.ps1 -StartupProfile ${TURBO_STARTUP_PROFILE || 'ornith-1.5'} -Detached -TextOnly`; `-TextOnly` makes the launcher skip `--mmproj`, so a normal start was text-only and the manual vision launch did not persist. There is **no start-on-demand** for `:8090`: it is started by `dev:gpu`, the `turbo:start*` scripts or `scripts/ensure-llama-server.mjs --spawn` (no callers found in `src`/package scripts); `--sleep-idle-seconds` exists in the installed build but is off and untested.
- **Change:** new opt-in `DEV_GPU_VISION=true` (read from the shell, `.env`, or `extra` like `DEV_GPU_ENABLE_MTP`). When set: profile defaults to `ornith-1.5-vlm` (an explicit `TURBO_STARTUP_PROFILE` still wins), `-TextOnly` is omitted, and `LLAMA_ARG_MMPROJ_OFFLOAD` defaults to `0` (projector on CPU; an explicit value in `.env`/shell wins), and the alias gate accepts `ornith-1.5-vlm` -> `ornith-1.5-9b`. After the alias check it reads `:8090/props` and prints a warning (non-fatal) if `modalities.vision` is not true, because the launcher can reuse an already-running text-only server. Default behaviour (flag unset) is byte-for-byte the previous argument list.
- **Verified:** `node --check` only. Not run: `dev:gpu` itself (it would start/replace the live `:8090`), and the reuse-vs-replace behaviour of the launcher when a text server is running was not exercised.

- Read the newest `screenshot_artifacts` row (SELECT only), resized the PNG to 512 px JPEG with `sharp`, and posted it to `:8090/v1/chat/completions` (image_url part, same shape as the caption script): HTTP 200 in ~17.9 s. ### Cause of the `openspec-controller.spec` failure found (2026-10-04, read-only run of the allowlisted command)
- Ran `node scripts/atlas/audit-nlp-agentic-error-readiness-v1.mjs --change parent-atlas-nlp-sidecar-feature-compiler` directly: status `NLP_AGENTIC_ERROR_READINESS_ENVIRONMENT_UNAVAILABLE`, `writesPerformed:false`, `mutationMode READ_ONLY_FIXTURE`, error `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'httpx'` (from `from httpx import ASGITransport, AsyncClient`). The audit (line ~109) does `spawnSync('python', ['-c', ...], { cwd: <repo>/python })`, i.e. whichever `python` is first on the PATH of the calling process, and that interpreter has no `httpx`. The live NLP sidecar itself is fine (`GET :8095/health` 200), so the sidecar environment exists but is not the interpreter the audit picks. The spec's `result.passed === true` therefore fails for an environment reason, not a code defect.
- **Not fixed, no install** (DEPENDENCY-CAPABILITY-GUARD-01: no `pip install` without a proven capability gap; the sidecar's own interpreter already has the dependency). Fix options for the owner: let the audit read an interpreter path from an env var (for example the sidecar's miniforge Python) instead of a bare `python`; or make the spec skip when the interpreter lacks `httpx`. The audit wrote its normal report to `.tmp/atlas-nlp-agentic-error-readiness-v1.json` (gitignored).

### VS Code tasks for the :8090 llama-server lifecycle + MCP decision (2026-10-04)
- **Audit first:** `.vscode/tasks.json` already had several start tasks (`⚡ TurboQuant: Start (text-only, :8090)`, `Start (vision + text)`, `Health Check`, the `Backend Stack` compounds) and `dev:gpu` on folder open; it had **no stop/unload task**. `git grep` over `src/mcp` found **no MCP tool** that starts, stops or unloads llama-server (only read-only status-style tools such as `trace_system_health`). The existing "vision + text" start task passes no profile or projector setting, so it does not give the CPU-projector launch.
- **Added (3 `process`-type tasks with argument arrays, so no shell quoting; `pwsh`):** `⚡ TurboQuant: Status (:8090 process, vision, VRAM)` (read-only: PID/start time/`--mmproj`, `/props` alias/vision/ctx, `nvidia-smi` memory), `⚡ TurboQuant: Stop (unload Ornith on :8090)` (graceful `Stop-Process` of only the process whose command line has `--port 8090`; never `taskkill /IM`, because Ollama's embedding runner shares the image name; never forces), `⚡ TurboQuant: Start (vision, CPU projector, :8090)` (`launch-turboquant.ps1 -Detached -StartupProfile ornith-1.5-vlm` with `LLAMA_ARG_MMPROJ_OFFLOAD=0`). `tasks.json` still parses (692 tasks); the Status task was executed read-only exactly as VS Code would run it (output: PID 36144, vision=True, n_ctx 65536, 7,700 used / 325 MiB free). **Stop and Start were NOT run.**
- **MCP decision (recommendation, not built):** do not expose stop/start/unload of `:8090` as an MCP tool for the model. The model that would call it is served by that same server, so a stop is self-termination mid-turn, and repo policy keeps operations typed and governed and forbids exposing process/Docker control to the model. If an MCP surface is wanted, make it **read-only status only** (alias, vision flag, ctx, VRAM free, with a `canonicalAuthority:false` receipt) and register it in the existing TRACE/ACP registry; lifecycle stays a human-run VS Code task. Nothing registered yet.

### Free-VRAM VS Code tasks + embedding runtime-truth gate (2026-10-04)

### cuVS KMeans bounded dry run in the WSL lane (2026-10-04; synthetic data, no DB, no writes; `:8090` was stopped for it)
- **Environment:** `/home/james/miniforge3/envs/atlas-rapids-cu13/bin/python` has torch 2.13.0, cupy 14.1.1, numpy 2.4.6, cuvs 26.6.0, pylibraft 26.6.0, psycopg 3.3.4 (the executor imports torch and cupy inside the function, so this env needs both).
- **Executor bug found (not fixed, repo executor untouched):** `python/atlas_compute/cluster_softmax.py::run_cuvs_soft_kmeans` fails at line ~140 on cuvs 26.6.0: `cuvs.distance.pairwise_distance` now returns a pylibraft `device_ndarray`, and `euclidean * euclidean` raises `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for *: 'device_ndarray'`. The fit itself succeeded; the failure is in the softmax/prediction batch. Fix for the owner: wrap the result with `cp.asarray(...)` before squaring. The dry run used a harness-only shim (scratch folder, `cuvs.distance.pairwise_distance` wrapped with `cp.asarray`); the module was not edited. Also noted: `cuvs` prints `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not callable` from `KMeansParams.__dealloc__` at interpreter exit (cosmetic).
- **Measurements (planted-cluster synthetic matrices, `input_normalization='l2_row'`, `max_iter=50`, seed `0xA71A5`, GPU otherwise free):** 5,000 x 768, K=32: 9.95 s wall (includes CUDA init), 2 iterations, 0 empty clusters, cluster sizes 132/156/188 (min/median/max), purity 1.0, max probability-sum error 1.2e-7, peak VRAM 1,531 MiB; 20,000 x 768, K=64: 11.1 s, 2 iterations, 0 empty clusters, sizes 279/312/359, purity 1.0, sum error 1.2e-7, peak VRAM 1,665 MiB (sampled with `nvidia-smi` every 0.4 s from outside; baseline during sampling 1,318-1,334 MiB, so the run added roughly 200-330 MiB over it). `canonical_authority False` in the receipt.
- **What this does and does not show:** the lane works and needs well under 1 GiB at these sizes, so the `KMEANS_MIN_FREE_MIB=4096` placeholder is conservative for 20k rows (it should be re-measured at the real row count, K and `max_iter`). It does NOT show behaviour on real `semantic_768` data (the synthetic clusters are well separated, so 2 iterations and purity 1.0 say nothing about real convergence, quality or timing), and it does not show coexistence with the `:8090` vision server (that leaves only ~200 MiB free, which is below even this small footprint).
- **State:** `:8090` is still DOWN. KMeans results were not written anywhere; no centroid owner decision was needed or made.

### EMBED-RUNTIME-READBACK-01 step 2: receipt binding built; the receipt does NOT cover Ollama's bytes (2026-10-04)
- **Finding (read-only, hashed locally):** Ollama's `embeddinggemma:latest` blob `~/.ollama/blobs/sha256-0800cbac9c2064dde519420e75e512a83cb360de3ad5df176185dc69652fc515` hashes to `0800cbac…` (the blob name is its content sha256), 621,867,104 bytes, `ollama show` quantization BF16. The committed EMB-PROV-01 receipt's artifact is `models/embeddinggemma-300m-f16.gguf`, sha256 `bc843658e96d2e9cc7c3402332b158f0cc4f73e61b23cef9a41acee1c0d372b7`, 621,867,360 bytes (the file the strict `:8081` llama.cpp lane loads). **They are different files** (different sha256 and 256 bytes apart). The receipt's `executorsAgree` rests on cosine 0.999988 between the two executors, which is parity evidence, not artifact identity. So the receipt proves the `:8081` lane's artifact; it says nothing about Ollama's bytes.
- **Added** to `src/lib/server/embedding/embedding-runtime-health-v1.ts` (+ spec, now 20 tests pass): `bindProbeToModelReceiptV1(probe, receipt, { maxAgeHours = 168, now, observedArtifactSha256 })` (pure; reads no files, no network). `llama-server` provider: binds the receipt's revision and 768 dimension only if the receipt is the v1 schema, `EMB_PROV_01_PROVEN`, checksum-consistent, fresh, recorded dim 768, live sha256 equals recorded revision, and `runtimeLoadedArtifact.loadedMatchesArtifact` is true; then a resident model classifies `MODEL_READY`. `ollama` provider: binds only if the caller supplies the observed blob sha256 and it equals the receipt's artifact; the real blob (a different file) stays `MODEL_IDENTITY_UNPROVEN` with both hashes in the reason. `cpu`: never bound. Stale/unproven/inconsistent receipt: binds nothing.
- **Consequence for the pipeline (decision for the operator, not made here):** persisted `semantic_768` embeddings should come from the strict `:8081` lane the receipt proves; Ollama is a parity-checked executor, not a proven-identical one. Whether Ollama's BF16 blob is acceptable for persisted vectors is a policy call; if yes, record it as an accepted second artifact (its own sha256 in a receipt) rather than letting parity stand in for identity.
- **Not done:** a `llama-server` probe (GET `:8081/props` loaded path vs the artifact, read at call time) so the `:8081` lane can reach a live `MODEL_READY` (the receipt's `runtimeLoadedArtifact` is a one-time readback, so today's binding is receipt-stale by design); the `:8097` Go-service probe/health semantics; wiring into `MODEL-RECEIPT-READBACK-01` or any caller. `:8081` is not running now.

### PERSISTED-SEMANTIC-768-AUTHORITY-01 (operator policy, 2026-10-04) + EMBED-RUNTIME-READBACK-01 step 3
- **Policy (frozen):** canonical persisted `semantic_768` writes require the receipt-bound strict llama.cpp `:8081` artifact (`bc843658…`, exact SHA-256, dimension 768, live loaded-path readback, recipe/input-policy binding). Ollama is a query-time / parity executor only: no canonical persisted writes; its state under the `:8081` receipt is `MODEL_IDENTITY_UNPROVEN`. Cross-executor cosine agreement (0.999988) is diagnostic evidence and MUST NOT satisfy artifact identity or persistence authority. Invariants: `executor parity != artifact identity != persisted-representation authority`; `semantic_768` = logical representation lane, `:8081` llama.cpp = admitted persisted executor, Ollama = alternate executor; executor agreement != executor interchangeability. If Ollama is ever admitted it needs its own `OLLAMA-EMBED-ARTIFACT-RECEIPT-01` (blob sha256 `0800cbac…`, 621,867,104 bytes, BF16, dimension, pooling, tokenizer and input-policy revisions, provider revision, runtime readback, recipe revision) and, by default, a DISTINCT representation revision until corpus-level equivalence is proven.
- **Step 3 built (commit `19da375e2e`, 31 tests):** `probeLlamaServerEmbeddingRuntimeV1` (GET `/props` + GET `/v1/models` only; observes loaded path, alias, `n_embd`; tolerates a `/v1` base URL) and a refined `bindProbeToModelReceiptV1` returning `artifactIdentityBound` / `dimensionBound` / `runtimeReady` as three separate facts instead of one bit. `MODEL_READY` = reachable + requested model resident + identity bound + dimension 768 + fresh receipt. For `llama-server`, identity needs the LIVE `/props` loaded path to equal the receipt artifact path and the live `n_embd` to be 768 (a resident alias cannot hide another file; the right alias on a different file gives resident but `MODEL_IDENTITY_UNPROVEN`; `:8081` serving the Ornith chat model gives `WRONG_MODEL_LOADED`). `runtimeReady` does not depend on the receipt. Probe, binder and classifier stay three separate functions. All against mocks; `:8081` is not running.
- **Status wording (operator):** `EMBED-RUNTIME-READBACK-01` = CODE_PROVEN / DEPLOYMENT_PENDING; `MODEL-RECEIPT-READBACK-01` = ARTIFACT_PROVEN / ACTIVE_RUNTIME_UNBOUND. Live proof still to do when it is operationally safe: start `:8081` -> read `/props` -> resolve the loaded path -> verify the exact SHA-256 of that file (the receipt's checksum is of the configured path) -> bind the receipt -> classify `MODEL_READY` -> only then resume recipe-qualified persisted `semantic_768` caller migration. The `:8097` Go health fix (`services/go-embedding-service`, `health_test.go`) is another session's uncommitted work and was not touched or verified here. Canonical embedding smoke command: `npm run atlas:embedding:caller-guard` (do not add ad hoc census invocations).

Result: wallMs 39,349, 29 iterations, inertia 8749.21, 0 empty clusters, sizes min 2 / p10 14 / median 315 / p90 517 / max 1,727, probability-sum error 1.5e-7, all finite, entropy 4.14. **Peak VRAM 1,968 MiB total, +391 MiB over the run's own minimum of 1,577** (242 -> 225 samples at 0.4 s).
- **Review (5,000 vs 20,000 real rows):** VRAM delta +252 -> +391 MiB (4x rows, +55%); a straight line through the two points is ~200 MiB fixed + ~9 KiB/row, which would put 55,169 rows near ~0.7 GiB and 219,259 rows near ~2.2 GiB. **That is a two-point extrapolation, not a measurement**; the corpus profiles stay `MEASURE_FIRST` and the next admissible measurement is 55k real rows. Wall time did not grow (40.9 s -> 39.3 s), so it is dominated by something other than row count (CUDA initialisation / kernel setup is the likely cause, not measured), while iterations rose 19 -> 29. Quality: the cluster-size skew persists (max/median 6x at 5k, 5.5x at 20k, smallest clusters 1-2 rows), consistent with K=64 being a poor choice for this data or with dense near-duplicate chunk groups; no clustering conclusion is drawn. The `CUVS_KMEANS_SMALL` profile (need 512 / min free 1024 MiB) holds for 20k real rows (+391 MiB). With Ornith's vision server up (~0.2 GiB free) the admission owner still would not admit it.
- **Not done:** 55k and full-corpus runs; a stratified (not lowest-id) cohort; any decision about K; wiring the VS Code Prepare task to the profile table.

### HYPERRAG-LINEAGE plan: packet -> packet incidence (operator design, 2026-10-04; nothing built; refines `HYPERRAG-INCIDENCE-OWNER-01`)
- **Audit (live, read-only):** no packet-incidence table or code exists (`information_schema` search for incidence/packet_edge/packet_neighbor/packet_relation/lineage returns only `atlas_packet_chunk_lineage`, which is packet->chunk, a different thing; `git grep` for `PacketIncidenceLineage` / `atlas_packet_incidence` is empty). Nothing to extend; this is a new layer.
- **Definition:** `PacketIncidenceLineageV1` = proof that packet A is connected to packet B in one exact Parent Atlas revision, by one known producer, from reproducible evidence. Fields: `schema atlas.packet-incidence-lineage.v1`, `packetKey`, `canonicalId`, `sourceRevision`, `neighborPacketKey`, `neighborCanonicalId`, `neighborSourceRevision`, `edgeType`, `workspaceRevision`, `graphRevision`, `producerId`, `producerRevision`, `evidenceRefs[]`, `inputChecksum`, `lineageChecksum`. `inputChecksum` = sha256(canonical_json of packet keys, source revisions, edgeType, workspace/graph revision, sorted evidence refs); `lineageChecksum` = sha256(canonical_json of the schema, both packet keys + canonical ids + source revisions, edgeType, revisions, producerId/Revision, sorted evidence refs, inputChecksum). Excluded from identity: DB row ids, timestamps, Qdrant/Neo4j internal ids, insertion order, executor ids.
- **Owners (no new service):** Postgres `atlas_packets` = packet identity; the admitted Graphify graph at one exact graph revision = structural evidence; `graphify_files` / packet lineage = source-revision binding; workspace revision = repository state. Graphify produces structural facts; the Parent Atlas identity resolver converts graph endpoints to canonical packets (Graphify never invents identity); the new Postgres owner `atlas_packet_incidence` (only if no suitable table exists; none does) stores the rows, unique on packet_key + neighbor_packet_key + edge_type + workspace_revision + graph_revision + source_revision + neighbor_source_revision + producer_revision; HyperRAG is consumer only and must not write. Do NOT use `atlas_hyperedges` (taxonomy edges, `packet_key` NULL) or the empty ontology tuple tables.
- **Tasks:** [ ] HYPERRAG-LINEAGE-01 freeze `PacketIncidenceLineageV1`; [ ] -02 name the Graphify projection stage as the single writer; [ ] -03 resolve both endpoints to exact canonical packets; [ ] -04 bind packet, neighbor, workspace, graph and source revisions; [ ] -05 require non-empty `evidenceRefs`; [ ] -06 deterministic `inputChecksum` and `lineageChecksum`; [ ] -07 write 20-100 canary rows only (about 10 packets, one frozen Graphify revision; needs a DB-apply decision, so gated by the Drizzle Safety Rule and explicit operator approval); [ ] -08 read back and verify identity, revisions and checksums; [ ] -09 HyperRAG consumes only exact-revision rows (`packet_key`, `workspace_revision`, `graph_revision`, `source_revision`), verifies the neighbour resolves at its exact revision, and returns `EMPTY_EXACT_REVISION_INCIDENCE` (valid) with no stale fallback; [ ] `MULTIHOP-FILL-01` first real packet -> packet -> packet expansion. Per-row proof: PACKET_A_RESOLVES, PACKET_B_RESOLVES, SOURCE_REVISION_A_EXACT, SOURCE_REVISION_B_EXACT, WORKSPACE_REVISION_EXACT, GRAPH_REVISION_EXACT, EVIDENCE_NONEMPTY, INPUT_CHECKSUM_VALID, LINEAGE_CHECKSUM_VALID; any failure = `LINEAGE_UNPROVEN`, no fuzzy fallback. Interacts with the open production-reachability finding: `search-runtime` must pass a revision snapshot before this writer is admitted.

### RUNNING PROOF 2026-10-04 (3): real-data cuVS KMeans at 55,000 rows (read-only; `:8090` still stopped) + a correction
- **Run:** same harness and rule at `LIMIT 55000` (last id `40035f3f-b1b9-4e58-8d5b-b58847d2bc47`), K=64, seed `0xA71A5`, read-only transaction `on`, `inputUniverseChecksum sha256:ede95f04dac1be1e1965df2dc40adc4849e4c25ad9e2bac593c911bd6d7a14d4`, `representationRevision` null, `writesPerformed:false`, `canonicalAuthority:false`. Result: wallMs 23,676, 33 iterations, inertia 23,727.7, 0 empty clusters, sizes min 3 / p10 88 / median 907 / p90 1,418 / max 2,413, probability-sum error 1.6e-7, all finite. **Peak VRAM 2,182 MiB total, +591 MiB over the run minimum of 1,591** (238 samples).
- **Review across sizes (real rows, K=64):** +252 MiB (5k), +391 (20k), +591 (55k): sub-linear (below the earlier two-point estimate of ~0.7 GiB at 55k). A three-point extrapolation puts the full corpus (~219k rows) near ~1.5 GiB; that is an estimate only, so `CUVS_KMEANS_CORPUS` stays `MEASURE_FIRST`. Wall time did not grow with rows (40.9 s, 39.3 s, 23.7 s), so it is not a row-count cost. 591 MiB exceeds the 512 MiB `CUVS_KMEANS_SMALL` need, so a measured `CUVS_KMEANS_MEDIUM` profile was added (need 768 MiB, minimum free 1,536 MiB); it is admitted at ~6.4 GiB free with the decoder off and not beside the Ornith server.
- **CORRECTION (my error, committed):** commits `6dd81f3708` and `5ee3cdde8c` shipped `gpu-workload-profiles-v1.ts` with an unescaped apostrophe inside single-quoted evidence notes ("the run's own minimum"), which is a TypeScript syntax error. The module could not be imported and the profile spec failed at transform time ("no tests"). The 7/7 pass recorded earlier was true only for the file before those note edits. Fixed in the next commit; `gpu-workload-profiles-v1.spec.ts` is 8/8. Lesson: re-run the spec after editing a string constant, not only after logic changes.
