### Step 1 result (2026-10-04, scratchpad harness `gsp53-harness-resolver.mts`, read-only, no DB writes)
- Cohort: 36 non-spec files in `atlas/indexing`, chunker lane `:8095`, resolver wired. **67 admitted edges** (all `type_ref`) of 2,886 raw candidates; 2,819 `UNRESOLVED_TARGET`; 0 collisions. Resolver split: `NOT_AN_IMPORT_BINDING` 2,555, `SPECIFIER_UNRESOLVED` 245 (targets outside the cohort), `RESOLVED_SYMBOL` 67, `NAMESPACE_OR_DEFAULT_UNPROVEN` 14, `EXPORT_NOT_FOUND` 5.
- Determinism: two builds in one process AND two separate processes gave identical `planChecksum` `sha256:0e60b088...2cde` and `edgeKeySetChecksum` `sha256:18e901ca...5df5`. Acceptance for step 1 met.
- **Root cause of the earlier zero:** the chunker's `symbol_nominations` never set `exported` (false even for `export function`), so the exports map was empty. Export status is derived from `parent_route`: `[export_statement, <decl>]`, or `[export_statement, lexical_declaration, variable_declarator]` for `export const`; a nested helper (`export_statement > function_declaration > variable_declarator`) is correctly excluded. Syntax only; this rule is now in the repo as `isTopLevelExportRouteV1` / `buildExportsForSourceV1` in `graphify-import-target-resolver-v1.ts` (spec 10/10); the harness itself is still scratchpad-only. Re-exports (`export { a as b }`) and default exports are not handled.
- **Source-side loss:** roughly half of the non-import facts (about 3,000 per build) have a source chunk that is not a nominated symbol (top-level fragments, `export` wrapper chunks) and are dropped, not admitted. Needs a rule for those chunks (probably the module-symbol DDL decision).
- Evidence spans are a labelled stand-in (`chunk_span_stand_in`, subject symbol byte range); step 2 (c) remains unbuilt. Nothing here is DB-apply evidence.

### Step 2 result (2026-10-04): line/column -> byte/row converter, evidence determinism proven in the harness
- Built `indexing/graphify-edge-span-converter-v1.ts` (+ spec 6/6; resolver spec still 10/10): `buildSourceLineIndexV1`, `convertChunkerSpanToByteRowV1` (pure; named failures `INVALID_SPAN_NUMBERS | LINE_OUT_OF_RANGE | COLUMN_OUT_OF_RANGE | END_BEFORE_START`). **Conventions measured against the live `:8095` chunker, not assumed:** lines 1-based, columns 0-based **Unicode code points** (emoji probe: `export` after `/* <emoji> */` reported at column 8; UTF-16 would be 9, UTF-8 bytes 11); output rows 0-based, bytes into the exact UTF-8 source.
- **The chunker's edge span is the CONTAINING DECLARATION node, not the reference site** (e.g. 14:0-20:2 covers a whole `type`). Evidence is therefore labelled `containing_node_span`, never a call-site span. A true reference-site span needs a different producer (ast-grep/LSP).
- **Gap (not fixed, lives in `packages/`):** the fabric's `treesitterChunkerXrefEdgeSchema` is strict `{src,dst,type,weight}`, so the adapter DROPS `evidence_start/end_line/column`; spans survive only on the raw sidecar `evidence.edges` (`from_evidence_key`, `to_evidence_key`). The harness joins facts to raw edges on `(xref_source_key, xref_target_key, xref_type)` (all facts joined; none unmatched). Carrying spans through the adapter is a canonical-contract change and needs its own decision.
- `reference_id` is built from `(src,dst,type,revision)` only, so edges with the same triple but different spans collide (230 duplicate fact ids collapsed in the cohort); the harness emits one candidate per raw edge so the planner merges them as evidence entries.
- Harness (scratchpad, 37-file cohort incl. the converter): 2,941 of 2,941 spans converted, **70 admitted**, 0 collisions; two separate processes gave identical `planChecksum` `sha256:610acb5f...5c62` and `edgeKeySetChecksum` `sha256:5b62b0ce...3ef4`. Evidence determinism proven for the harness; still no DB write and not apply evidence. Still open: the roughly half of non-import facts whose source chunk is not a nominated symbol, and re-exports/default exports.

### Step 3 (first pass, 2026-10-04): cross-directory cohort = whole `src/lib/server/atlas` tree (790 of 791 non-spec `.ts` files), read-only
- Same scratchpad harness, resolver + span converter, `:8095` chunker. **2,276 admitted unique edges** (`call` and `type_ref`) from 43,528 candidates over 166,502 raw sidecar edges; 12 evidence entries merged (2,288 admitted candidates -> 2,276 edges); 0 collisions. Two separate processes gave identical `planChecksum` `sha256:ccfa4b44...cc80` and `edgeKeySetChecksum` `sha256:ddf933d1...3617`. Cross-directory imports DO resolve (the 36-file single-directory run admitted 70).
- Resolver split (one build): `NOT_AN_IMPORT_BINDING` 37,021 (locals/builtins), `SPECIFIER_UNRESOLVED` 3,174 (packages, aliases outside the tree, files outside the cohort), `RESOLVED_SYMBOL` 2,276, `EXPORT_NOT_FOUND` 688 (likely re-exports/default/`export *`; not classified), `NAMESPACE_OR_DEFAULT_UNPROVEN` 265, 3,579 import facts used only as bindings. Admission rate is about 5% of candidates; this is the honest ceiling of import-binding-only resolution, not a defect.
- **Source-side loss unchanged and now measured at scale:** about 49,700 facts per build had no nominated-symbol source chunk and were dropped (about 53% of non-import facts). Needs the module-symbol / unnominated-chunk decision before any DDL.
- One file failed: `route-feature-map.ts` was rejected by the sidecar (`422 string_too_long`, body `source` limit 200,000 characters). Large files need chunked submission or a raised limit; not handled.
- **Contract break to resolve (another session's change, not touched):** `sveltekit-frontend/src/lib/server/nlp/miniforge-nlp-sidecar.ts` (uncommitted, parallel session, CSGR-3) now passes `occurrence_positions` through on every edge, but the `.strict()` `atlasAstEvidenceEdgeSchema` in `packages/parent-atlas/src/core/treesitter-chunker-evidence-adapter.ts` has no such key, so `compileGraphifyStructuralIntelligence` throws a ZodError on live `:8095` evidence. The harness strips the key before compile as a workaround. The schema needs an additive optional `occurrence_positions` (or the adapter must strip it) before this lane works unmodified.
- **Opportunity:** 119,803 of 166,502 raw edges (72%) carry non-null `occurrence_positions`. These are probably true reference-site positions, which would replace the `containing_node_span` evidence with call-site evidence. Semantics (absolute vs chunk-relative, code points vs bytes) are NOT yet verified; see CSGR-3 (`python/prove_occurrence_positions_live_wiring.py`).
- Still not DB-apply evidence. Next per this section: GSP-5.4 DDL as a reviewed paper design (no apply), after the unnominated-source and `occurrence_positions` decisions.

### `occurrence_positions` semantics verified (2026-10-04, read-only, 200 files incl. 80 with non-ASCII text)
- Source of the field: sidecar CSGR-3 (`python/atlas_structural_provenance.py::find_occurrence_positions` + `occurrence_to_absolute_position`, wired in `miniforge_nlp_sidecar_v2.py::add_edge`): each entry is a file-absolute `[line, column]` START POINT (not a span) found by re-parsing the chunk; `None` when the name was not found.
- **Reference-site validity:** 69,892 positions checked (28,974 edges whose target is a name); the source text at the position starts with the edge target name in **69,892 of 69,892** cases (0 misses). These are real reference sites, unlike the chunker's containing-declaration spans.
- **Conventions:** lines 1-based (indexed as `line - 1`), columns 0-based. **Column unit looks like UTF-8 BYTES, not code points:** 69,888 positions match both readings (nothing non-ASCII before them); of the 4 that discriminate, 4 match only as bytes and 0 only as code points. The discriminating sample is small (43 positions on non-ASCII lines), so treat as probable, not proven. This DIFFERS from the chunker's `evidence_*_column` (code points, measured earlier); the two must never share a converter default.
- **Multiplicity:** 13,698 of 28,974 edges (47%) carry more than one position. One logical edge with many occurrences maps onto `planGraphifyEdgeReplayV1`'s one-edge/many-evidence-entries model, so each position should become its own evidence entry.
- A reference-site span would be start point + the target name's length in the same unit (dotted names such as `path.join` cover the whole text). NOT built: needs (1) the schema decision above (additive `occurrence_positions` in `packages/parent-atlas`), (2) a byte-column variant of the converter, (3) a decision on which edges lacking positions (28% of raw edges) fall back to `containing_node_span`.

### GSP-5.4 concrete paper DDL (2026-10-04, reviewable draft; NOTHING written or applied; supersedes the column list in the earlier HOLD NOTICE where they differ)
- **Gate status:** the earlier condition for drafting ("at least one real file yields admitted edges") is now met in the harness (2,276 admitted edges over the whole `atlas` tree, deterministic across processes). The other gates (rollback plan, Drizzle review, explicit operator apply approval) are NOT met; this entry does not authorize any DB write.
- **Baseline is the applied `drizzle/manual/20260903_graphify_symbols_edges_additive_v1.sql`** (`graphify_edges`: `edge_id`, `workspace_id`, `subject_symbol_id`, `predicate`, `object_symbol_id`, `unresolved_target`, `evidence_kind`, `evidence_span jsonb NOT NULL`, `confidence`, `source_revision`; indexes on subject and `source_revision` only). The LIVE table, its row count and whether any legacy rows exist were NOT probed in this pass; a read-only `\d graphify_edges` and `count(*)` are required before review.
- **Corrections to the earlier note, with reasons:** (1) NO `evidence_checksum` on the parent: evidence is deliberately outside edge identity and can grow, so a NOT NULL parent checksum would go stale or force mutation; evidence is a child set with a per-entry hash. (2) NO supersession columns now: a newer admitted `graph_revision` yields a different `edge_key`, so revision scoping already supersedes; add an explicit tombstone state only if a real need appears. (3) The new columns are NULLABLE with a CHECK that makes an admitted row (edge_key set) complete, so legacy rows are never relabelled as admitted.
- **Parent evidence columns:** the existing `evidence_kind`, `evidence_span`, `confidence` are NOT NULL on `graphify_edges`. Decision for review: mirror the primary (lowest-sorted) evidence entry into them at insert and treat the child table as the full set; the alternative (relax them to nullable) changes an applied contract.
- **Evidence content:** `reference_site` entries need the `occurrence_positions` decisions recorded above (additive package schema key, a byte-column converter); until then evidence is `containing_node_span` only.
- **Insert path:** one transaction: `INSERT INTO graphify_edges ... ON CONFLICT (edge_key) WHERE edge_key IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING RETURNING edge_id` (look the id up on conflict), then `INSERT INTO graphify_edge_evidence ... ON CONFLICT (edge_id, evidence_entry_hash) DO NOTHING`. Replaying the same fact adds evidence, never a second edge. Persist through the existing `graphify-symbol-writer-v1.ts`, not a new writer (GSP-5.5).
- **Rollback / pre-image (additive, so rollback is cheap):** before apply capture `SELECT count(*), count(edge_key) FROM graphify_edges` and a `pg_dump -t graphify_edges -t graphify_symbols`; canary rows are tagged by `execution_id` and removable with one `DELETE ... WHERE execution_id = <canary>` (evidence cascades); full rollback = `DROP TABLE graphify_edge_evidence`, drop the new indexes and constraint, `ALTER TABLE graphify_edges DROP COLUMN` the eight new columns (no legacy data touched).
- **Drizzle Safety Rule findings:** (a) `drizzle.config.ts` `tablesFilter` contains NO `graphify_*` pattern (0 matches) and `graphify_edges`/`graphify_symbols` are not declared in `src/lib/server/db`, so a `drizzle-kit generate/push` could try to DROP them; add `'!graphify_*'` (or declare them) BEFORE any related migration work, as its own reviewed change. (b) This is a manual sidecar migration (needs a `sidecar-migrations.json` entry with `appliedBy`/`appliedAt` after approval; not in the journal). (c) Do not run `drizzle-kit push`.
- **Still open for review:** the parent-mirror vs relaxed-NOT-NULL choice; whether `execution_id` should be a typed FK to the graphify execution ledger (`graphify_executions`) instead of text; the unnominated-source rule (about half of non-import facts are dropped before any of this); and the `WORKSPACE-REVISION-KEY-MIGRATION-01` gate.

```sql
-- GSP-5.4 PAPER DESIGN. NOT a migration: do not save under drizzle/ or drizzle/manual/ until reviewed and approved.
-- Additive only; every new column is NULLABLE so existing rows stay observable but non-admitted (no backfill, no invented revisions).
ALTER TABLE public.graphify_edges
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS edge_key               text,   -- 'sha256:' || 64 hex, from planGraphifyEdgeReplayV1 (graphify-edge-replay-identity-v1.ts)
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS workspace_revision_key text,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS graph_revision         text,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS producer_id            text,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS producer_revision      text,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS execution_id           text,   -- reference to the graphify execution ledger; metadata, never the arbiter
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS target_source_revision text,
  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS observed_at            timestamptz DEFAULT now();  -- metadata only

-- The arbiter. Partial, so legacy rows (edge_key IS NULL) never collide.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS graphify_edges_edge_key_uq_v2
  ON public.graphify_edges (edge_key) WHERE edge_key IS NOT NULL;

-- An admitted row is complete or it is not admitted at all.
ALTER TABLE public.graphify_edges ADD CONSTRAINT graphify_edges_admitted_complete_v2 CHECK (
  edge_key IS NULL OR (
    edge_key ~ '^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$'
    AND workspace_revision_key IS NOT NULL AND graph_revision IS NOT NULL
    AND producer_id IS NOT NULL AND producer_revision IS NOT NULL
    AND target_source_revision ~ '^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$' AND source_revision ~ '^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$'
    AND object_symbol_id IS NOT NULL AND unresolved_target IS NULL)) NOT VALID;  -- VALIDATE after review; legacy rows have edge_key NULL so it passes

-- Selectors and reverse traversal (only subject is indexed today).
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS graphify_edges_snapshot_v2 ON public.graphify_edges (workspace_revision_key, graph_revision) WHERE edge_key IS NOT NULL;
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS graphify_edges_object_symbol_v2 ON public.graphify_edges (object_symbol_id) WHERE object_symbol_id IS NOT NULL;

-- Evidence is OUT of edge identity and grows (47% of edges have several occurrences), so it is a child set, not a parent column.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.graphify_edge_evidence (
  evidence_id          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  edge_id              uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.graphify_edges(edge_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  evidence_entry_hash  text NOT NULL,                    -- sha256 of the canonical entry (evidenceEntryOfV1)
  evidence_kind        text NOT NULL,                    -- 'reference_site' | 'containing_node_span' | ...
  start_byte bigint NOT NULL, end_byte bigint NOT NULL,  -- UTF-8 byte offsets into the exact source revision
  start_row integer NOT NULL, end_row integer NOT NULL,  -- 0-based
  confidence real NOT NULL CHECK (confidence >= 0 AND confidence <= 1),
  evidence_refs jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
  CONSTRAINT graphify_edge_evidence_span_v2 CHECK (end_byte >= start_byte AND end_row >= start_row),
  CONSTRAINT graphify_edge_evidence_entry_uq_v2 UNIQUE (edge_id, evidence_entry_hash));
```

### GSP-5.3B OCCURRENCE-POSITION-SEMANTICS strict gate + SOURCE_CHUNK_UNBOUND classification (2026-10-04, read-only; supersedes the looser check above where they differ)
- **Gate (100 files: 40 with non-ASCII text, 60 ASCII; each file chunked TWICE):** per edge type, every position's source text starts with the target name, with identifier boundaries on both sides, inside the source line, inside the edge's containing declaration span, identical across the two runs, and no `source_revision` mismatch.
  | Edge type | edges | positions | exact token | boundary violations | out of bounds | outside containing span | unstable | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | REFERENCES | 9,715 | 19,130 | 19,130 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `REFERENCE_SITE_PROVEN` |
  | CALLS | 6,526 | 12,532 | 12,532 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `REFERENCE_SITE_PROVEN` |
  | EXPORTS | 858 | 587 | 587 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `REFERENCE_SITE_PROVEN` |
  | IMPORTS | 484 | 3 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `POSITION_SEMANTICS_UNPROVEN` (n=3; 481 of 484 have no positions) |
  | DEFINES | 1,769 | 0 | - | - | - | - | - | no positions (expected) |
- Edges WITHOUT positions: REFERENCES 2,303 (19%), CALLS 540 (8%), EXPORTS 288, IMPORTS 481, DEFINES 1,769; these can only carry `containing_node_span` evidence. Multiplicity: REFERENCES 3,816 edges and CALLS 2,562 edges have several positions; same-line repeats occur (3,298 and 1,601), so each position must be its own evidence entry. No multiline names.
- **What is and is not proven:** the field gives a START point only; the end is start + the target name's length in the same unit. Token-start, boundaries, bounds, containment and run-to-run stability are proven; exact end position is derived, not provided. The column unit is still only PROBABLY UTF-8 bytes (4 discriminating positions, all byte-only). The verdict applies to the CALLS/REFERENCES/EXPORTS populations, not to IMPORTS.
- **Schema decision (made, and already applied by the parallel session as an UNCOMMITTED edit):** additive optional `occurrence_positions` (nullable tuple, line positive, column non-negative) on the canonical strict `atlasAstEvidenceEdgeSchema` in `packages/parent-atlas/src/core/treesitter-chunker-evidence-adapter.ts`. Verified here: with that edit, live `:8095` evidence compiles through `compileGraphifyStructuralIntelligence` with NO stripping (13 edges with positions, 21 reference facts, `COMPILED_NONPROMOTABLE`). The harness workaround is no longer needed. Still open: the fabric's `xref_edges` (`src`/`dst`/`type`/`weight`) drops the positions, so they do not reach reference facts; packages `dist/` was not rebuilt.
- **SOURCE_CHUNK_UNBOUND (corrected):** my first pass joined edge sources on `upstream_node_id` only and read the wrong field (`symbol_name`; the raw chunk field is `name`), so its numbers were invalid and are discarded. Corrected, over 22,057 non-IMPORTS edge sources (99 files, every 8th file): `SOURCE_NOMINATED` 6,163 (27.9%); `EXPORT_WRAPPER_ONE_NOMINATED_CHILD_BY_BYTE_RANGE` 4,473 (20.3%); `EXPORT_WRAPPER_MULTIPLE_NOMINATED_CHILDREN` 328 (1.5%); `EXPORT_WRAPPER_NO_NOMINATED_CHILD` 1,508 (6.8%); `UNNOMINATED fragment|variable_declarator` 5,082 (23.0%); `UNNOMINATED function|arrow_function` 1,140 (5.2%, anonymous: the name belongs to the enclosing declarator); `UNNOMINATED import|import_statement` 3,363 (15.2%).
- **Why export wrappers are the source:** the chunker emits a separate `export_statement` chunk (kind `export`, no symbol id) whose id the edges use, plus a distinct declaration chunk inside it that IS nominated; the fabric picks the first chunk per id, so edges bind to the unnominated wrapper. A deterministic, syntax-only recovery candidate: when an `export` wrapper contains exactly one nominated chunk by byte range, use that chunk as the subject (would recover about 20% of edge sources; the 1.5% multi-child case needs a direct-child rule via `parent_route` depth). NOT implemented: it changes subject binding in the canonical fabric (`packages/`), so it needs an owner decision. Variable declarators (`const x = ...`, 23%), anonymous arrows (5%) and import wrappers (15%) are not nominatable kinds today; making them symbols is the separate module/const-symbol decision. Keep them as `SOURCE_CHUNK_UNBOUND`; do not force them into symbol edges.
- **`SIDECAR_BODY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`:** `route-feature-map.ts` (over 200,000 characters) is rejected by the sidecar with 422; recorded, not an architectural blocker.
- **GSP-5.4 impact (no DDL change needed):** the child evidence table already models this. `reference_site` rows come from `occurrence_positions` (start_byte from the byte column, end_byte = start + UTF-8 byte length of the name); `containing_node_span` is the fallback where positions are absent (19% of REFERENCES, 8% of CALLS). `occurrence_count` is `count(*)` of `reference_site` rows per edge, derived, not stored. IMPORTS evidence stays `containing_node_span` until a larger IMPORTS sample exists.

### GSP-5.3B addendum (2026-10-04): targeted cases pass; positions are DUPLICATED, which corrects earlier counts
- **Targeted synthetic cases (all exact, read-only):** aliased import `import { helper as h, type Cfg }` gives REFERENCES positions on BOTH `helper` (1:9) and `h` (1:19); `import * as ns` gives `ns` (2:12); a multiline call `h(` + args on later lines points at the callee token (6:12); two same-line calls `h(1) + h(2)` give 5:12 and 5:19; member calls `ns.compute` point at the full dotted token (10:9, 10:28). Type references inside a signature (`c: Cfg`) can have NO position (REFERENCES `Cfg` under the function chunk had `[]`), so some type refs stay `containing_node_span` only.
- **Duplicates inside one list:** each real occurrence can appear twice in a single edge's `occurrence_positions` (e.g. `h` at 5:12, 5:19 and 6:12 each listed twice). Measured on 99 files: 37,713 raw positions but 33,355 distinct (11.6% in-list duplicates; 2,924 of 16,043 edges with positions carry at least one). **Correction:** edges with several positions are 7,717 by raw count but only 5,409 (34% of edges with positions) by distinct count, so the earlier "47% multi-occurrence" figure overstated multiplicity; any `occurrence_count` MUST be computed over DISTINCT positions.
- **The same reference site is emitted by sibling edges:** 33,355 per-edge site entries collapse to 16,046 distinct (edge type, target name, position) triples, so each reference site appears on about 2.1 edges (the same call is attributed to the `export` wrapper chunk, the declaration chunk and enclosing chunks, each with its own containing span). Consequences: (1) evidence entries must be de-duplicated by (kind, byte span) per logical edge, which `planGraphifyEdgeReplayV1` already does by canonical entry (the 12 merged entries in the cohort run); (2) subject attribution matters, because one site should belong to the INNERMOST nominated declaration, which is the same fix as the `export`-wrapper byte-range rule recorded above; (3) per-edge counts here must not be read as distinct reference sites.
- Verdicts unchanged: `REFERENCE_SITE_PROVEN` for CALLS/REFERENCES/EXPORTS; IMPORTS still `POSITION_SEMANTICS_UNPROVEN` (aliases and namespace imports behave correctly in the synthetic probe, but the real-file IMPORTS sample is 3); missing positions stay `DECLARATION_SPAN_ONLY`.

Committed with the 10 MB per-file limit respected. Left out on purpose: 220 files over 10 MB (mostly 95 MB `docs/reports/openspec-evidence*/census-v1.json`), 120 per-run snapshot files under `docs/reports/openspec-evidence/pipeline-*` (164 MB, regenerable), nested untracked directories (`claude-mem`, `granite-docling-258M`, `models/embeddinggemma_300m`) and `simd-bridge/cpp/build-x64-cuda/*` build output. Three tracked files still contain a hard-coded DB DSN with a dev password in a fallback (`scripts/atlas/build-mcp-tool-manifest-packets.mjs`, `scripts/atlas/register-orphaned-chunks.mjs`, `sveltekit-frontend/src/lib/server/analysis/worker.ts`): pre-existing in HEAD, not introduced now; move to env-only in a hardening pass (credentials belong in `.env.local`).

## 18. ARTIFACT-AUDIT plan (large artifacts / analysis pipeline): reconciled with existing owners, kept SEPARATE from the HyperRAG/GSP-5 edge work (2026-10-04; nothing run, no code)

Operator-supplied review (L0 file/artifact classification, L1 extraction, L2 canonical storage + embedding, L3 graph/analysis derivations, L4 cache/GPU/serving) recorded as ARTIFACT-AUDIT-01..15. Duplication-prevention rule applied first: most steps already have an owner, so the plan EXTENDS those; it does not add an auditor, a classifier, a scheduler or a sidecar. HyperRAG (section 17 start-here) stays the priority; this audit must not create another architecture fork.

### Existing owners found (verify before building anything)
- **Inventory + classification:** `scripts/atlas/audit-artifact-bloat.mjs` -> `docs/reports/artifact-bloat-report.json` (generated 2026-09-07: 26,763 files, 31,098 MB; kinds raw_json 22,317, report 2,561, duplicate 1,352, ndjson 443, embedding_checkpoint 65, parquet 12, duckdb 5, som_checkpoint 8; 484 duplicate groups; row keys relPath, sizeBytes, kind, sha256, duplicate, recommendation) and `scripts/atlas/apply-artifact-tier-classifications.mjs` -> `docs/reports/artifact-tiering-application.json` (last applied 2026-06-19 from an earlier report; decisions `keep_canonical` 6,721 / `move_to_cold` 51 / `compress_zstd` 89 / `index_metadata_only` 6 / `delete_if_regenerable` 1,378; policy flags `destructiveActionsPerformed:false`, `coldMoveRequiresRestoreProof`, `deleteRequiresCanonicalPeer`, `activeRuntimeModelsProtected`; writes a metadata manifest only, never moves or deletes). Related: `audit-model-artifacts.mjs`, `audit-offline-vector-artifacts.mjs`, `audit-docker-storage-retention-v1.mjs`, `audit-current-graph-artifact-readiness-v1.mjs`, `audit-openspec-report-disposition-v1.mjs` (advisory 10 MB), the archive rule and `docs/archive-manifest.json`.
- **The real gap in L0:** the bloat report predates the OpenSpec evidence censuses (2026-10-01..04): 95 MB `docs/reports/openspec-evidence*/census-v1.json` files (many), 120 per-run snapshot files (164 MB) under `docs/reports/openspec-evidence/pipeline-*`, and 220 uncommitted files over 10 MB in the worktree on 2026-10-04. Those run artifacts (GENERATED_EVIDENCE / PIPELINE_RUN) are not covered by the existing kinds.
- **JSON parse path:** `sveltekit-frontend/src/lib/server/gpu/simdjson-bridge.ts` (`fastJsonParse`, native simdjson via `tensorrt_bridge.node`, V8 `JSON.parse` fallback, payloads under 1 KB bypass native). simdjson is CPU SIMD, not GPU. It already exists; the open question is only whether a STREAMING path for 10-100+ MB files is worth it.
- **Graph parity:** NetworkX CPU oracle vs cuGraph is ALREADY proven on a frozen snapshot (`sveltekit-frontend/docs/reports/graph-snapshot-parity/receipt.json`: 162,234 nodes / 108,156 edges, PASS, correlation 1, ARI/NMI 1.0). ARTIFACT-AUDIT-12 is therefore a RE-RUN on a `graphify_edges`-derived snapshot after GSP-5 admits edges, not new work.
- **Ontology:** real oaklib already runs as `python/atlas_oak_kernel.py` (:8095, change `parent-atlas-ontology-kernel`) with a layered TypeScript resolver; ARTIFACT-AUDIT-14 is satisfied by that layering unless a concrete ontology workload shows a gap. OAK stays ontology-only: never packet lineage, never code-graph identity, never PageRank or ANN.
- **Ownership + sidecars:** `npm run atlas:audit:ownership` (runtime-ownership registry and baseline) answers ARTIFACT-AUDIT-13; a new peer owner is a failure.
- **GPU scheduling:** the proposed `AnalysisJobV1` (CPU_ONLY / GPU_SMALL / GPU_MEDIUM / GPU_CORPUS) already maps onto `gpu-workload-profiles-v1.ts` (`CUVS_KMEANS_SMALL/MEDIUM/CORPUS` MEASURED, others MEASURE_FIRST) routed through `decideGpuMemoryAdmissionV1()`. Extend the profile table (add a CPU-only class if wanted); do NOT add a parallel scheduler.
- **Embedding authority:** persisted `semantic_768` only from the receipt-bound strict `:8081` GGUF (live readback `MODEL_READY`, entry 6); no batch job may switch embedding provider silently. Large artifacts are never one embedding each: extract semantic units first, then bind canonicalId, sourceRevision, workspaceRevisionKey (the admitted `sha256:` run key), representationRevision, embeddingModelRevision, inputChecksum.
- **ACE wire format:** already ruled (project CLAUDE.md): ACE packets are bounded descriptors with artifact refs, never inline bulk; heavy evidence is referenced by artifactRef + artifactChecksum + recordCount + schema + producerRevision + workspaceRevisionKey.

### The 15 steps mapped (O = existing owner to extend, G = genuine gap)
1. **AUDIT-01 inventory >10 MB + pipeline-run artifacts:** O `audit-artifact-bloat.mjs`; refresh READ-ONLY and extend its kinds (G: add GENERATED_EVIDENCE / PIPELINE_RUN / immutable evidence).
2. **AUDIT-02 classify (SOURCE, GENERATED, EVIDENCE, CACHE, MODEL, UNKNOWN; and canonical source / reproducible generated / immutable evidence / cache / model-binary / manual review):** O `apply-artifact-tier-classifications.mjs` decisions; map the review vocabulary onto the existing decisions instead of a second classifier.
3. **AUDIT-03 JSON/JSONL measurement (size, record count, max record, parse time, peak RAM):** G, small read-only benchmark on the 95 MB censuses using `fastJsonParse` vs a streaming parse.
4. **AUDIT-04 reproducible-and-should-not-be-Git-tracked:** O tiering policy (`deleteRequiresCanonicalPeer`, `coldMoveRequiresRestoreProof`); archive, never delete.
5. **AUDIT-05 canonical storage (Git / Postgres / object-cold store / model registry):** O archive manifest + SeaweedFS (canonical object store) + `models/model-manifest.json`; G: write the per-class decision table.
6. **AUDIT-06 semantic extraction unit per artifact class:** G; ties to the embedding recipe census (EMB-RECIPE-03) and the packet/chunk grain.
7. **AUDIT-07 EmbeddingGemma provenance + 768 writer:** DONE for the runtime lane (entry 6); persisted-write caller migration still pending.
8. **AUDIT-08 pgvector/Qdrant duplicates and identity/revision payloads:** O known findings (5,634 duplicate PG mappings in `codebase_chunks_768`, 15 duplicate canary targets, `_768_v2` lacking canary rows, EMB3A challenger NOT_PROMOTED); re-census live before citing.
9. **AUDIT-09 ACE packets embedding heavyweight payloads:** O wire-format rule; G: a read-only scan for packets with inline bulk.
10. **AUDIT-10 graph outputs canonical vs projection vs cache:** O frozen ownership (Postgres/`graphify_edges` canonical; Neo4j navigation mirror; NetworkX oracle; cuGraph executor; HyperRAG consumer).
11. **AUDIT-11 simdjson benchmark on representative large JSONs:** G, only after step 3 shows parse time or RAM is a real problem; the bridge exists, add nothing before measuring.
12. **AUDIT-12 NetworkX vs cuGraph parity on one frozen snapshot:** O existing PASS receipt; re-run on a `graphify_edges` snapshot after GSP-5.
13. **AUDIT-13 Python/FastAPI/NLP sidecar duplicate ownership:** O `npm run atlas:audit:ownership`.
14. **AUDIT-14 OAK only against real ontology workloads:** O `atlas_oak_kernel.py`; no new work.
15. **AUDIT-15 one consolidation receipt:** G, last.

### Guardrails (carry into any implementation)
No new dependency before the census (DEPENDENCY-CAPABILITY-GUARD-01). Do not feed 10-100 MB evidence files to EmbeddingGemma, Graphify or Git indiscriminately. Large JSON: stream, filter fields, normalize, hash, batch-insert or write an artifact receipt (avoid read-whole-file then `JSON.parse`). LangExtract/LLM extraction stays behind deterministic extraction (grounded spans required, enrichment candidates only, never canonical without promotion). PyTorch is for learned representations (autoencoders, routing classifier, reranker), not ETL or graph ownership; dense deterministic math prefers cuBLASLt/cuVS/cuGraph. FastAPI stays a typed CPU/NLP boundary; RAPIDS stays on its dedicated WSL2 GPU path.
Status: PLANNED. First action when picked up: refresh `audit-artifact-bloat.mjs` read-only and diff its kinds against the 2026-10-01..04 OpenSpec evidence artifacts.

### REVIEW pgvector/semantic-cache/LUT proposal against existing owners (2026-10-04, read-only audit; nothing built)
Proposal reviewed: Postgres+pgvector as canonical/exact oracle with Drizzle, a pgvector `semantic_cache` (query -> nearest cached query -> reuse an ACE artifact), an `agent_tool_lut` routing table, `.okf` schemas/manifests/receipts, Pydantic/Zod mirrored from one schema, SearXNG as external evidence, no Chroma. Verdict per piece (owner check order: `packages/` first, then `sveltekit-frontend/src`, `scripts/atlas`, `drizzle/manual`):
- **Aligned, nothing to add:** no Chroma (one `semantic_768` lane, many executors, matches the ownership rule); Qdrant = retrieval projection, cuVS = GPU executor, TurboVec = compact challenger; Node/SvelteKit control plane with Python only for Python-owned analysis; compact numeric codes only as derived routing keys never carrying meaning (`ResidencySortKeyV1` family already follows this).
- **SearXNG: owner exists.** `retrieval/search-observation-v1.ts` and `retrieval/search-acquisition-routing-v1.ts` (discovery is not acquired evidence; SSRF/allowlist via `validateExternalUrl`; submission goes through the existing `requestAcquisition()`), plus the `kag.web_search` MCP tool whose real routing to SearXNG is still the open item `MCP-WEB-01`. The proposal's "SearXNG -> evidence candidates -> admission/provenance -> ACE" matches that contract; do not add a second search adapter.
- **Semantic cache: three different things, only one exists.** (1) `embedding/semantic-embedding-cache-v2.ts` + `drizzle/manual/20260830_semantic_embedding_cache_v2.sql` is a text->vector cache with an exact revision-qualified key (`halfvec(768)`), status `planned_sidecar`, UNAPPLIED, no similarity search; it is not the proposal's cache. (2) Exact, revision-qualified artifact caches already exist: `atlas/cache/ace-bitfrost-cache-identity-v1.ts` (`AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1`, built on `ContextManifestV2`), `ace/cache-keys.ts` retrieval identity V3, and the Redis exact-hash lane in `ace/query-router.ts`. (3) Similarity reuse of admitted answers is already served at the gateway by the Bifrost L2 semantic cache (Qdrant-backed, threshold 0.8). A pgvector `semantic_cache` would therefore be a SECOND similarity-reuse owner; per the One-Canonical-Owner rule it needs an explicit classification decision against Bifrost L2 first. If built, its guards must reuse `AceBitfrostCacheIdentityV1`/`ContextManifestV2` fields instead of inventing a parallel `workspace_revision_key/graph_revision/context_manifest_checksum` set, and a near-duplicate query must not reuse a legal-domain answer by default.
- **Query-embedding recipe is undecided.** A cache keyed on query embeddings bakes in the recipe, and `EMBED-CALLER-CONVERGENCE-01` (prefix-aware adapter; `/api/embed` applies no EmbeddingGemma task prefix) is still open, as is the Ollama phase-out (replacement embedding backend not chosen). Do not build similarity reuse before the recipe is frozen.
- **The example `atlas_embeddings` table does not exist and must not be created.** Real surfaces: `codebase_chunk_index.content_embedding` (halfvec 768) and `content_embedding_768` (vector 768) are the embedding truth; `atlas_embeddings_768` / `_384` / `_64_latent` exist in `drizzle/manual/20260716_atlas_knowledge_layer.sql` with ZERO TypeScript callers under `src`; `_384` is a retired lane. A chunk-level embedding joins to a packet through chunk->packet lineage, not `USING (packet_key)`. The part worth keeping is the revision-qualified filter (`representation_id`, `representation_revision`), which `codebase_chunk_index` lacks today (a populated column does not prove lineage).
- **`agent_tool_lut`: genuinely absent, with real overlap.** No table or TS registry matches (searches returned nothing). Existing routing: `scripts/atlas/runtime-mcp-tool-selector.mjs` (Qdrant `tool_manifest` + domain signals, registry fallback, `tool_manifest` currently empty so different queries can return the same subset), `selectMcpToolSubset()`, `ACPToolRegistry.ts`, and the lane order in `ace/query-router.ts`. A deterministic domain x intent -> permitted-tool table fills the empty-manifest fallback gap, but it must be registered as the fallback owner of that selector (and in `ACPToolRegistry.ts`), not a peer router; enabled/priority/cost columns and a revision policy are reasonable, an LLM-free default is the point.
- **`.okf` already exists in two shapes, neither matching the proposed layout.** Repo-root `.okf/` holds `concepts/*.yaml` (gap concepts), `domains/*.yaml`, `docs/`, `index.md`; `sveltekit-frontend/.okf/` holds `manifest.json`, `packets/packets.ndjson`, `features/`, `functions/`, `tools/`. The proposed `schemas/`, `manifests/`, `receipts/` subdirectories do not exist; the contract owner is `atlas/contracts/okf-cross-domain-v1.ts` plus `scripts/atlas/audit-okf-runtime-ownership.mjs`. Extend those, keep `.okf` records as pointers with checksums (not payloads), and re-run the ownership audit after any structural change.
- **Zod vs Pydantic:** both exist independently (FastAPI sidecars validate with Pydantic; `miniforge-nlp-sidecar.ts` validates by hand and package contracts use Zod); there is no single frozen source or generator between them. The proposal's "mirror from one schema" is a real gap, and the repo's `proto:from-zod` script (Zod -> protobuf) is the nearest existing mechanism to evaluate before adding another; not verified here.
- **Decision requested before any build:** (1) classify a pgvector similarity-reuse cache against Bifrost L2 (extend, replace or reject); (2) freeze the query-embedding recipe; (3) approve or reject the tool LUT as the registry-fallback owner. No migration, table or file was created.

## 19. ROUTER registry / query-control fabric: owner audit, ROUTER-01 built, work map (2026-10-04)
Context: the proposed fabric is NLP normalization -> routing LUT -> initial retrieval -> frontier/deque -> bounded multihop expansion -> promotion/ranking -> ACE packet -> synthesis, with GPU executors on numeric candidate batches and QLoRA strictly offline. The layering is consistent with the repo's Wire Format Layering Rule (meaning != encoding != transport != executor). This entry records what already exists, what was built, and what each proposed work item maps to.

### Owner audit (read-only; `packages/` first, then `sveltekit-frontend/src`, `scripts/atlas`)
No numeric `domain_id` / `intent_id` / `lane_id` registry existed. The string vocabularies are FRAGMENTED, with different meanings, and must not be merged by assumption:
- **Lane vocabularies (at least 4):** `SearchLaneSchema` in `retrieval/search-contract.ts` (lexical, dense, sparse, topology, authority, routing, documentation, temporal, memory); `retrievalLaneSchema` in `retrieval/cognitive-router.ts` (bm25, ann, som, graph, inverse, all); the RF7 `CanonicalFusionLane` / `retrieval/retrieval-lane-aliases.ts` equivalence classes (owns only dense and lexical so far; `RF7-CONTRACT-PARITY-01` is the open convergence step); executor class names in `retrieval/search-lanes.ts` (`Bm25Lane` is actually pg_trgm and its string is a live routing key); plus the RPC packet lane descriptors in `atlas/contracts/rpc-packet-registry-lanes-v1.ts`. `SearchLaneSchema` is used only inside `search-contract.ts`.
- **Intent vocabularies (3 different axes):** `routerIntentSchema` in `cognitive-router.ts` (debug, locate, graph, topology, semantic); `detectQueryIntent` in `ace/search-router.ts` (query SHAPE: symbol, concept, code); the atlas-tools `classify_intent` MCP result (TASK: repair, research, planning, with its own coarse domains retrieval/graph/agent-workflow/frontend/database/general). The example intents in the proposal (TRACE_OWNER, GRAPH_ANALYSIS, CURRENT_PUBLIC_LAW, SEARCH) are NOT registered anywhere.
- **Domain vocabularies (3):** `CANONICAL_DOMAINS` in `atlas/domain-taxonomy.ts` (auth, ui, retrieval, network, database, cache, agent, graph, ml; version `parent-atlas-domain-taxonomy-v1`); `DomainClassificationV1.domainId` strings qualified by `taxonomyRevision` (navigation evidence, never identity); the 13 top-level groups of `atlas_domain_ontology` in Postgres; plus the atlas-tools coarse domains above. A `LEGAL` domain id (as in the proposal's example) is in none of the in-process enums.
- **A deterministic intent -> lanes router already exists and is UNWIRED:** `retrieval/cognitive-router.ts` exports `routeQuery`, `getLaneExecutionOrder`, `shouldParallelExecute`, `buildRetrievalPlan`, `RouterOutput` (intent, lanes, topK, clusterBudget, readFiles, reason, confidence). It has NO importers in `src` or `scripts` (only census outputs mention it) and no spec. Related routers with different jobs: `router/deterministic-tool-ranker.ts`, `router/viterbi-router.ts` (challenger-only), `scripts/atlas/runtime-mcp-tool-selector.mjs` (tool selection), `ace/query-router.ts` (lane order for ACE packets).

### ROUTER-01 built (registry only; no router, no LUT, no new vocabulary members)
- `atlas/contracts/routing-registry-v1.ts` (+ spec 14/14): frozen numeric codes over the four IN-PROCESS enums (`search_contract.lane` 9 members, `cognitive_router.lane` 6, `cognitive_router.intent` 5, `canonical_domains` 9). Rules: a code is a routing key only (never identity, never persisted without vocabularyId + revision); codes are scoped PER vocabulary with no cross-vocabulary mapping; code 0 is reserved and never routable; valid codes 1..255; APPEND-ONLY (a removed/renamed member keeps its code as `deprecated`, a new member gets the next unused code).
- The spec imports the real source enums and fails in BOTH directions (a live source member without a code, or a live registry entry whose name the source no longer has), so source drift cannot silently shift routing.
- NOT registered (reason): `detectQueryIntent` and the atlas-tools `classify_intent` (not enums in-process), the `atlas_domain_ontology` groups (database rows), the RF7 fusion lanes and executor class names (alias work still open). The Postgres/`.okf` mirror of this registry is a later step.

### Work-item map (the proposed order, against existing owners; status as of this entry)

| ROUTER-01 registries | `routing-registry-v1.ts` | built (above) |

No migration, table, `.okf` file or LUT was created.
